Liam Neeson's New Action Thriller Officially Removed From Almost 1,000 Theaters After Just 1 Weekend

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ever since Taken transformed Liam Neeson into an unlikely action superstar, the Oscar-nominated actor has spent nearly two decades proving that age is no obstacle when it comes to beating up bad guys. From Non-Stop and The Commuter to The Ice Road, Neeson has become one of Hollywood's most dependable names for audiences looking for a straightforward thriller. Unfortunately, that reputation hasn't always translated into box office success, particularly as mid-budget action movies have increasingly found their audiences on streaming, and his latest might need that to happen again.

Neeson's new action thriller The Mongoose was officially dropped from 826 theaters after just one weekend, falling from 1,014 locations to only 188 following a disastrous $464,421 domestic opening. The Samuel Goldwyn Films release debuted on October 30, 2026, averaging just $458 per theater during its first three days.

The situation didn't improve during its second weekend, when The Mongoose earned only $79,842, representing an 83% decline. Its domestic total currently stands at approximately $777,000, making it a particularly disappointing result for a movie that initially received a release in more than 1,000 theaters.

The collapse is especially striking given Neeson's history of carrying action thrillers to respectable theatrical returns. However, the film's modest opening suggested that audiences simply weren't turning out for his latest adventure, leaving exhibitors with little reason to retain it.

When war hero Ryan “Fang” Flanagan (Neeson) accidentally kills his ex-wife’s abusive new fiancé in self- defense, he knows how it will look. Accused of a crime he didn’t commit and with nothing to lose, Fang steals a Shelby Mustang and makes a desperate bid for freedom. As he leads the police in a cross-country car chase, he is aided by his former Special Forces Army comrades. When the chase sparks the interest of local news channels, Fang ends up closely monitored on national TV with a sympathetic public rooting for his safe getaway.

The film stars Liam Neeson (Taken) as Ryan "Fang" Flanagan, Marisa Tomei (Spider-Man: No Way Home) as Tara, Ving Rhames (Mission: Impossible) as Tanker, Michael Chiklis (The Shield) as Pope, and Marcus Thomas (The Forger) as Munch. The movie is directed by Mark Vanselow.

The Mongoose opened in US theaters on October 30, 2026. A streaming premiere date has not yet been announced. Stay tuned to Collider for all the latest news and updates on the biggest and best movies and shows heading to your favorite streaming platforms soon.

Netflix's 8-Part Female John Wick Series Is One Of Its Best Binges

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It’s rare for any thriller to live up to the John Wick franchise, let alone a small-screen show, but one Netflix series with a single season managed this feat. It is tough to think of an action thriller movie from the last 15 years that was more influential than 2014’s John Wick. In terms of pure plot, the Keanu Reeves vehicle was the simple story of a retired assassin killing every criminal involved in his dog’s death.

However, in visual and aesthetic terms, John Wick spelled the death knell for the shaky cam style that dominated action thrillers in the 2000s and early 2010s thanks to the popularity of director Paul Greengrass’s groundbreaking Bourne sequels. After John Wick’s release, the movie’s sequels popularized a hyper-smooth, slick action filmmaking style that prioritized gliding, unbroken Steadicam shots over fast-cutting.

Soon enough, Atomic Blonde, Bullet Train, Extraction, the Nobody movies, Kate, Gunpowder Milkshake, Pretty Lethal, Violent Night, and almost every other action thriller movie had embraced this CGI-assisted filmmaking style. Few of these efforts could match the John Wick sequels, and even John Wick’s own streaming spinoff The Continental ended up feeling like a knock off rather than a legitimate franchise entry. This is why it is so impressive that Netflix’s under-seen South Korean thriller series My Name recaptured John Wick’s entire appeal perfectly.

As an action thriller about revenge, My Name sounds straightforwardly similar to John Wick in terms of plot. However, the complicated lore of the show’s story makes My Name closer in story terms to the Internal Affairs movies or their Americanized re-imagining, The Departed. Han So-hee’s Yoon Ji-woo is the daughter of a powerful mob member who joins the city's police so she can act as a mole and pass on information to the Dongcheon drug cartel.

Yoon Ji-woo’s boss, the kingpin Choi Mu-jin, encourages her to spy on the police, meaning the show’s heroine soon lands a spot on the very task force that is investigating the cartel. However, once there, she learns from her new detective partner that Choi Mu-jin is suspected to have killed her father. With split loyalties, Yoon Ji-woo has no one to trust and every reason to sharpen her combat skills. While My Name’s plot is as complex as any crime drama like Love/Hate, this show is no slow burn, realistic character study.

Instead, Netflix’s hyper-stylized My Name is a neon-drenched thrill ride, as Yoon Ji-woo battles her way through the city’s criminal underworld while trying to solve the mystery of her father’s sudden death. Gritty, dark, and fast-paced, the action show is a must-watch for fans of revenge thrillers.

Every action show needs to find the right balance between constant fights, chases, and gun battles, but My Name’s tense premise gives the story an incredible head start in this regard. My Name’s twisty plot and the complicated machinations of its heroine’s plan mean that even the show’s quiet dialogue scenes crackle with constant tension.

There is always a risk that Yoon Ji-woo will be exposed as a mole in either organization, which ramps up tension during quiet scenes and makes the action all the more hard hitting. Even compared to dark crime thrillers like This City Is Ours, My Name’s one-season story is a masterclass in tension building.

On that note, the show’s one-season status actually works for, rather than against, My Name as a series on the whole. While it would have been hard to drag My Name’s story out for more than one season, Netflix’s killer John Wick replacement wisely limited its story to just one perfectly paced outing instead.

RHOP’s Karen Huger Shares Update on Ray Huger Marriage After Season 11

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Real Housewives of Potomac's Karen Huger exclusively told E! News at the Hunting Wives season two premiere where she stands with husband Ray Huger as they plan the holidays with daughter Rayvin.

Karen Huger has no illusions of grandeur about the future of her marriage.

While the Real House of Potomac's Grand Dame admitted viewers would have to stay tuned to see how she and husband Ray Huger continue to work on their relationship during season 11, she confessed issues with her partner of 32 years haven't gone away.

"We're going through a rough patch," Karen exclusively told E! News at the Hunting Wives season two premiere Nov. 12, crediting her ability to face their struggles to the fact that "Ray and I have always been honest."

And despite the difficulties in her relationship since her return home after serving six months in prison due to a 2024 DUI conviction, the reality star has a positive outlook about their family unit with daughter Rayvin Huger.

As Karen explained, "The one thing is, we will always be a family, and we will always be friends."

And because of the couple's unbreakable bond, their plans for the holidays remain a family affair where the motto is the more the merrier.

"We're going to celebrate as a family," Karen shared. "In fact, we are going down to Virginia, near the guest house that I just renovated, to celebrate with our entire family."

As for how the 63-year-old feels about her equally complicated relationship with costar Wendy Osefo?

"I'm hoping for the best for Wendy. I'm always in her corner," Karen remarked. "We are supporting her 100 percent and wishing for the best."

Aside from her Bravo friend group, Karen is also a big fan of the women of Maple Brook, Texas, with the second season of Hunting Wives arriving on Netflix Nov. 26. In fact, she effused about her favorite actress in the drama, Brittany Snow.

As Karen put it, "Brittany Snow? Love, love, love. Sign me up. I'm ready to kill someone, OK."

With that in mind, Karen is open to trying out her acting chops alongside the Hunting Wives cast, musing when it comes to a future cameo, "All things are possible."

3 Best Movies To Watch On Prime Video This Weekend (November 14-15)

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

From that lineup, only the latter is currently trending on the streaming service nationwide, as the post-apocalyptic thriller Apocalypse Z: New World currently stands as the 9th most popular movie on Prime Video's Top 10 Movies chart in the United States. As such, it remains a good option as we head into the weekend, just like the first film in the franchise, Apocalypse Z: The Beginning of the End.

Last weekend's recommendations were a comedy, a psychological thriller, and a post-apocalyptic thriller. This time around, I have shifted the list towards sci-fi, with one of the films being a masterpiece cyberpunk release that stands as one of the most iconic sci-fi movies to have ever been released. Joining it is an action thriller with a sci-fi twist to it.

The only non-sci-fi movie on our new Prime Video list is a spy comedy, which promises to be the film for the whole family on the streaming service this weekend. These are the best movies to watch on Prime Video this weekend.

Starting the list is the most different release, Agent Cody Banks, the spy comedy starring Frankie Muniz and Hilary Duff, which is like a teenaged James Bond adventure. Given how the casting of Daniel Craig's replacement as James Bond is the talk of the town in Hollywood, with actors like Robert Pattinson and Nicholas Hoult joining the race, I felt that Agent Cody Banks was a timely choice.

That said, do not expect the film to go as dark and gritty as many of the 007 movies, given how Agent Cody Banks relies heavily on Muniz's comedic timing from his experience in the sitcom Malcolm in the Middle. This one is worth watching for the leads' chemistry, the spy gadgets, the fast-paced action, and the light take on material like James Bond.

Infinite starts to lean our list towards sci-fi, with the Mark Wahlberg release currently standing as the most popular movie on Prime Video in the United States. Infinite is the perfect option for those who are looking for action this weekend, but want something grittier and a lot more mature than Agent Cody Banks.

Infinite follows Wahlberg as Evan McCauley, a man who deals with mysterious visions, has abilities that he does not remember training to develop, and more. However, when he learns from a group, the Infinites, that these visions are memories of past lives, everything changes, and he must face a dangerous foe. Infinite is not groundbreaking, but the film is worth watching for its intense action and talented ensemble, which also includes Dylan O'Brien, Chiwetel Ejiofor, Jason Mantzoukas, and more.

Finally, Blade Runner: The Final Cut was one of the early October arrivals on Prime Video, and anyone who follows the masterpiece sci-fi cyberpunk franchise started by director Ridley Scott must know why it is a timely movie. After all, Prime Video's Blade Runner 2099 series will be released on the streaming service on November 25, so now is the time to start the franchise or rewatch it, if you have not already.

This first Blade Runner movie set up an immersive and intricate world where the line between human and machine is blurred. Harrison Ford delivers a magnetic performance in the role of Rick Deckard, and the film helped set the tone for live-action cyberpunk movies and TV shows. All those factors make the morally complex thriller a must-watch on Prime Video this weekend.

3 Elite Prime Video Series to Binge This Weekend (Nov 14-15)

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

2026 has been a strong year for Prime Video, but thoughts are now turning to 2027 as the cold nights set in and Christmas approaches. Recently, two of the streamer's most popular shows announced their 2027 release dates. Off Campus Season 2 will debut on April 28, 2027, and Benito Skinner's acclaimed comedy Overcompensating will return on February 17, 2027. But before thoughts turn entirely to 2027, there's still plenty more to be enjoyed before the year ends. With that in mind, here's a look at three must-watch shows on Prime Video this weekend.

For more recommendations, check out our list of the best shows and movies on Prime Video.

Is it possible to recommend anything this weekend without mentioning The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power? The smash-hit fantasy series began with an acclaimed outing in 2022, before stumbling with a divisive second installment. Earlier this week, the show returned for a third installment, now five years after the fall of Eregion and with a terrifying war between the Elves and Sauron (Charlie Vickers) in full swing.

"The Rings of Power Season 3 successfully balancing what fans know will happen alongside the journey that will take them there is what makes for good TV," wrote Collider's Therese Lacson in her review of Season 3, one of several that were full of praise. Morfydd Clark, Robert Aramayo, Owain Arthur, Sophia Nomvete, Ismael Cruz Córdova, and more all star in the third season, which continues with two episodes on November 18.

As mentioned previously, it was recently announced that the hit 2026 series Off Campus ​​​​​​will return in April next year. With that in mind, whether you're new to the show or could benefit from a rewatch, why not binge all eight episodes this weekend? Based on the novels by Elle Kennedy and adapted for the screen by Louisa Levy, the series follows music student Hannah (Ella Bright) and star hockey player Garrett (Belmont Cameli) as a "fake relationship" blossoms into reality.

For fans of romance shows like Bridgerton and Heated Rivalry, this is a must-watch indulgence. Called "exactly what romance novel lovers want from a TV adaptation" in Collider's review of the series, Off Campus is bursting with heart and drama, with each episode sure to keep you hooked. Bright and Cameli are perfectly cast as Hannah and Garrett, although the main romantic focus of Season 2 shifts away from the pair.

If you've been enjoying Ben Watkins' new series The Greatest, all about the early life of Muhammad Ali, on Prime Video, then you simply can't miss his most famous production, Cross. Aldis Hodge stars as the unorthodox detective Alex Cross from the James Patterson novels, as his complicated and fascinating personal and professional lives combine as tricky crimes are solved.

Don't expect a light mystery series with Cross, as this gritty, psychological thriller will demand your full attention. The series stars Isaiah Mustafa and Juanita Jennings alongside Hodge, and has released 16 episodes so far across two seasons. Amazon MGM Studios renewed the series for an eight-episode third season, which is expected in spring next year, so make sure you catch up in time.

James Gunn Says “No One From Skydance Has Seen” ‘Man Of Tomorrow’: “There Are A Lot Of Untrue Rumors Going Around”

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

James Gunn is clearing up some rumors following his exit as co-head of DC Studios.

With Skydance making changes amid the Paramount and Warner Bros. merger, Peter Safran and Gunn announced they would not be heading DC Studios.

Amid their exits, many rumors have circulated. One prominent story was that Skydance saw the Superman sequel, titled Man of Tomorrow, and planned to make some edits, but Gunn has shut down those allegations.

“There are a lot of untrue rumors going around, but one of them is that Skydance has seen Man of Tomorrow and that all sorts of sweeping changes are being made,” Gunn posted on Threads.

He continued, “No one from Skydance has even seen the movie – the only people that have are Peter Safran and a very small trusted group of friends and family. So any stories around this narrative are false.” Watch on Deadline

Man of Tomorrow wrapped filming in August, with Gunn sharing a joyful post and celebrating everyone involved in the project.

“That’s a wrap on principal photography for Man of Tomorrow,” Gunn shared on Threads. “Truly a joyful experience, and I have immense gratitude to my cast and crew.”

RELATED: ‘Constantine’ Director Feels “More Hopeful” For Sequel Following James Gunn & Peter Safran’s DC Studios Exit

Man of Tomorrow is set for release on July 9, 2027, with David Corenswet reprising his role as Clark Kent. The cast also includes Nicholas Hoult as Lex Luthor, Lars Eidinger as Brainiac, Rachel Brosnahan as Lois Lane, Skyler Gisondo as Jimmy Olsen, Sara Sampaio as Eve Teschmacher, Isabela Merced as Hawkgirl, Nathan Fillion as Guy Gardner, Edi Gathegi as Mister Terrific, and Adria Arjona as Maxima.

Aaron Pierre, who plays John Stewart in HBO’s Lanterns, is set to jump to the big screen in the Superman sequel. Also set to reprise his Blue Beetle role is Xolo Maridueña, who will play the superhero once again since the 2023 film, directed by Ángel Manuel Soto.

Amid the executive shakeup at Skydance, former Marvel exec Jeremy Latcham is now set to run DC Studios.

Drew Lock has another limited practice but does not have an injury designation

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Drew Lock was limited in practice Friday, his sixth consecutive practice without team drills. Like last week, though, Lock will play through oblique and elbow injuries.

Lock does not have an injury designation for Sunday’s game against the Raiders.

“He had a similar cadence and workload in terms of last week,” Seahawks coach Mike Macdonald said Friday, via John Boyle of the team website. “It’s good to have done it and gotten through the week well. I know he’s confident and optimistic, so we’re in good shape.”

The Seahawks ruled out starter Sam Darnold (ribs/quad) for another week, but he would return after the Week 11 bye.

Offensive guard Quenton Nelson (knee), offensive tackle Charles Cross (ankle) and defensive tackle Leonard Williams (stinger) also won’t play.

The Seahawks are getting back outside linebacker Uchenna Nwosu, receiver Rashid Shaheed and nose tackle Brandon Pili. All three were full participants Friday and do not have an injury designation.

Trump Says Federal Film Tax Credit Is Gaining Momentum

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The proposal for film and TV tax incentives, which drew broad bipartisan support when it was introduced earlier this year, faces an uphill battle for passage in the lame-duck session.

After a sweeping loss at the midterms for Republicans dimmed the short-term prospects of a federal film and TV tax credit, President Trump is pushing for passage of legislation for production subsidies.

Trump on Friday said he’s working with lawmakers on both sides of the aisle on the legislation.

“The support and popularity of doing this, and bringing Entertainment back to the United States of America, has been unbelievable!” Trump wrote on Truth Social.

A bipartisan group of lawmakers has introduced proposed legislation to establish a federal film and TV tax credit, aimed at reshoring production to the U.S. amid a tit-for-tat battle among countries to host Hollywood.

If passed, the Motion Picture, Television, and Entertainment Revitalization Act would establish a 20 percent base tax credit on labor expenses, rising to a maximum of 30 percent with bonuses. Only projects with a budget over $1 million, where 75 percent of filming occurs in the U.S., would be eligible for the credit. The subsidy would be stackable on top of individual tax incentives from states, much like the system in several countries that’ve lured production away from the U.S. in recent years.

In Friday’s post, Trump said that the entertainment industry has “largely moved to Canada, the United Kingdom, Australia and other countries.”

“The actors, producers, and all others involved want to come back home!” Trump added. “Importantly, hundreds of thousands of Blue Collar Workers will be positively affected, BIG TIME, by the passage of this Bill.”

The post follows Democrats preparing investigations into the Trump administration, raising questions about whether they will focus on film subsidies during the lame-duck session. The tax credit would cost billions of dollars, money that Democrats may be hesitant to attach to a short-term bill during the year-end budget fight.

Still, Trump stressed that Democrats are open to the legislation since “much of the money being lost to other countries is coming out of the Blue States.”

“The amount of money spent on these Tax Incentives will be made up tenfold by the money pouring into the Treasury’s coffers,” he added. “Meetings are being set up, routinely, with the Leaders of both Parties in order to, GET THIS DONE!”

Grey’s Anatomy’s 17-Year Heartbreak Story Legitimizes Its New Meredith

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Warning! SPOILERS about Grey’s Anatomy season 23, episode 5 ahead.A story entirely unrelated to Meredith unexpectedly introduced the perfect replacement for the Grey’s Anatomy protagonist in season 23, episode 5. Ellen Pompeo scaling back her appearances in Grey’s Anatomy season 19 raised questions about whether an existing character on the ABC medical drama would take over from Meredith for the kind of storylines that focused on her, both in terms of her centrality to several storylines and how she always propelled drama and important themes in them. Over the years, Grey’s Anatomy proposed several Meredith replacements, but they rarely worked perfectly.

Meredith appearing several times throughout each season at Grey Sloan Memorial made finding some kind of replacement superfluous, especially as her visits managed to coincide with either momentous developments in her research and the hospital, or disasters. However, in the day-to-day of Grey Sloan Memorial’s happenings, some characters channeled the Grey’s Anatomy protagonist more than others. Among them were two of Meredith’s mentees, Jo and Simone, who both suffered a lot in their past, had a complicated history with Grey Sloan Memorial, and proved their resilience just like Meredith did before them.

However, Grey’s Anatomy season 23, episode 5 may have just introduced another unlikely contender in the contest for Meredith’s replacement, one whose story might not completely fit within Meredith’s parameters, but who had his fair share of family drama. The arrival of his father at Grey Sloan Memorial in Grey’s Anatomy season 23, episode 4’s ending and what that caused in him made his similarities with Meredith impossible to ignore.

Grey’s Anatomy season 23, episode 5 doesn’t even reveal the name of Benson Kwan’s biological father, but the effect he has on him is evident. Kwan doesn’t want anything to do with him, and yet after learning a little more about him, he still offers to test for compatibility for a potential bone marrow transplant, as a way to help him with his myeloma. Only Jules overhearing his private call reveals that the only reason he had come to Grey Sloan Memorial was to convince Kwan to help him.

Grey's Anatomy season 23's premiere's plane collision highlights a recurring problem of the medical drama that risks decreasing its stakes.

While bearing some differences, including how despicable Kwan’s father is for wanting to simply manipulate his son to save himself, Kwan’s dynamic with his father in Grey’s Anatomy season 23, episode 5 is eerily similar to Meredith’s with Thatcher in season 6. In Grey’s Anatomy season 6, episode 4, Thatcher arrived at Seattle Grace with liver failure. While Lexie wanted to help him, Meredith didn’t want anything to do with him, and yet it was Meredith who was compatible.

Thatcher didn’t want to accept Meredith’s generosity because he didn’t believe he deserved it, proving a better parent than Kwan’s father. Still, Meredith was eventually swayed by Lexie’s request rather than caring about Thatcher’s fate specifically. Kwan might still decide to help his father, although how they left things makes it extremely unlikely. Even if he didn’t, though, his willingness to help his father despite all the hurt that he caused him makes him extremely similar to Meredith Grey, both in his decision and family dynamic with his father.

Meredith didn’t leave a lasting impact on Kwan’s training in the little time they were both at Grey Sloan Memorial. However, Grey’s Anatomy highlighted time and time again the similarities between Kwan and Meredith’s values and professional histories. After all, Kwan was so burned by having to pay for his mother’s medical bills well after her death that he suggested his patient and her husband divorce to help with the insurance. Meredith went to greater extremes by committing insurance fraud in Grey’s Anatomy season 16, but it’s clear they came to the same conclusion that their system was broken.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

In her long career at Grey Sloan Memorial Hospital, Meredith did so many illegal and unethical things in a bid to help her patients. Among them was messing with Derek’s trial so that Adele received the real drug and not the placebo to help Webber. Kwan being fired for injecting his patient with something the FDA had recommended against for his patient only made his similarities with Meredith more evident, in addition to setting up Kwan’s redemption as Grey’s Anatomy season 23’s biggest story.

These key aspects of Kwan’s story thus make him the most similar to Meredith for the characteristics that count among his resident group. Hopefully, if he still finds himself coming to grips with his father’s horrible requests when Meredith is at Grey Sloan Memorial, Meredith can guide Kwan to move on in Grey’s Anatomy season 23, serving as a full-circle moment for her and Kwan, too.

Vampire Santa Claus Is Coming to Town in Ilja Rautsi’s Charades-Sold ‘Red Snow,’ Which Debuts Clip: ‘It’s a Gruesome Christmas Present’ (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In the story, teenage Johanna dreams of a glittering Twilight vampire prince. Instead, she gets Martti: a balding, beer-bellied 49-year-old bloodsucker who slips into a Santa suit to gain entry to homes and devour entire families. Soon, she finds herself trapped in a bloody and horrific nightmare of ageing masculine toxicity, family trauma, and festive slaughter. Related Stories Aspyn Ovard on Her Divorce, Being in a Throuple and Joining ‘The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives’ Universe: ‘I Hope People Can Understand Me a Little Bit Better’ How to Watch ‘Southern Charm’ Season 12 Online: The Bravo Reality Series Returns Tonight

Sold by Charades and produced by Bufo, Allfilm and Hobab, “Red Snow” debuts exclusive clip here:

Mixing vampire mythology with Santa Claus – “and the sexual undertones that come with it” – has surprised some viewers, admitted Rautsi.

“It can be disturbing to people, but it’s not a disturbing film. It’s set in this absurd, outrageous world that hopefully allows me to get away with it. That’s the part of its charm: the grotesque and all the twists and turns in the story.”

After Fantastic Fest and Sitges, “Red Snow” is now heading to Tallinn Black Nights Film Festival and has been at the AFM. Hannu-Pekka Björkman, Anni Iikkanen, Tommi Korpela, Kari Hietalahti and Marja-Liisa Nguyen star.

It was important to still ground the film in some “recognizable emotion,” stated Rautsi.

“These are heightened characters, but there are also some glimmers of what they are actually going through. We don’t necessarily have to root for them all the time, but we’re along for the ride – despite all the madness.”

Christmas can be a stressful time, with or without vampires.

“For me, this juicy Christmas setting acts as a boiling kettle. We’ve all been there. We know the feeling of spending Christmas with family when you’re supposed to be jolly but it’s actually miserable,” he laughed.

“You can see this in all of Shane Black’s films that are set at Christmas but not about Christmas. I think they have affected me, too.”

Rautsi doesn’t hide his affection for horror. Known for shorts “Helsinki Mansplaining Massacre” and “The Night of the Living Dicks,” he has also written “Nightborn” and “Hatching.”

“I’m a nervous person. A good horror film is my comfort zone, though, because it validates the internal life of someone who’s afraid. Fear is such a taboo subject – people don’t talk about it. But then we go and see a horror film, and we celebrate the fact that it’s O.K. to be afraid. It can be fun to experience it in a safe setting,” he said.

Despite its wild premise, “Red Snow” captures how he perceives life. It can be scary, but it can also make you laugh.

“I’m constantly laughing about all the things I’m afraid of. The goal of the film was to express how I experience things. You can be going through something ridiculous and then notice something beautiful about the whole situation. Life can be so many things, all at once, within the space of five seconds.”

10 Classic Rock Albums To Listen to Before Kicking the Bucket

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

​​​​​​Joining the choir invisible. Shuffling off this mortal coil. Ceasing to be. Biting the dust. Wearing wooden pajamas. Vacating the earthly meat prison. No matter how it's said, it all means the same thing: death. Death is the great equalizer, the one thing that is inevitable regardless of one's race, religion, income or social status. Every day brings us closer to hopping the twig.

But hey, you're still on this side of the grass, right? Unless you're a well-read zombie, but that seems unlikely (but not impossible... discuss). So you have a choice to make: sit idly by, paralyzed by the thought you may have had your last Slurpee; or make the most of the time you have left. And if — hopefully — you've selected the latter, before kicking the bucket you've got things you want — nay, need — to do, see, and hear. Thankfully, your friends here at Collider have compiled a list that speaks to the latter: 10 rock albums you need to listen to before going belly up, ones that are not only awesome but offer guidance as you prepare for the inevitable. We're dying, literally, to share those with you now.

There comes a point in everyone's life where it's time to drop the charades and get real. That means raw, emotional honesty with others and yourself, and there is no better album that wears emotional honesty on every track like Fleetwood Mac's 1977 classic Rumours. The story behind the album is legendary at this point, recorded as the interpersonal drama among the group was akin to a soap opera, with separations, outright spite and heartbreak, making the atmosphere in the studio, shall we say, tense. A lesser band would have buried that under upbeat pop/rock songs, recording in separate sessions if needed. Not Fleetwood Mac: they owned that energy and put it all on vinyl, creating a timeless masterpiece that is relatable, real, and, in the case of "Don't Stop," even hopeful.

Getting real with ourselves potentially opens up a proverbial Pandora's Box of questions about life, death, time, money, or even one's mental health. While it may not provide any answers to these questions, Pink Floyd, with their iconic album Dark Side of the Moon, sure as all hell wax philosophical about those same themes. Like Rumours, the album has an emotional honesty that resonates only with the fluid cohesion that comes from being a concept album. As a result, Dark Side of the Moon is less a record than it is an experience, one that should be made before going up to the "Great Gig in the Sky."

Speaking of experiences, seeing KISS in concert is one that is an absolute must. Or at least was, given the band gave their last performance at Madison Square Garden on December 2nd, 2023. So if that's on your bucket list, you now only have two options: wait for the KISS virtual avatars to hit town, or listen to their 1975 album Alive! Opt for the latter. Their breakthrough album, consisting of cuts from their first three albums (KISS, Hotter Than Hell and Dressed to Kill), achieves something that other live albums have mostly failed to accomplish: the feeling of being there. Alive! captures the bombastic, take-no-prisoners energy of a KISS live show and puts it on vinyl. Twice, that is, with that raw power requiring containment on a double album. Good luck replicating that, avatars.

The "when" of kicking the bucket is assured. The "how," on the other hand, is another story. There are millions of ways one could go, from horribly tragic to an eternally looping end as a Darwin Award winner. You could, as alluded to in the aforementioned Rumours, even "Go Your Own Way." And if that's the case, as Van Halen cheerfully suggests on their 1984 album, you "might as well jump" (but in all seriousness, please don't — help is always available). The last album with long-time frontman David Lee Roth (until 2012's A Different Kind of Truth) is also, arguably, their best, with "Panama," "Hot for Teacher," "Drop Dead Legs" and "Jump" the standouts. Really, though, the album is so good it doesn't really matter where you "jump" in.

It's ironically fitting that the last album the Beatles recorded before their demise, one that includes "The End," would show up on a list like this, no? As Collider explains, Abbey Road is arguably the greatest record of the 20th century. It's high praise, and even if you may disagree — you shouldn't, it's pretty damn good — then it still stands tall amongst the crowd. Noted as much for its iconic album cover as its tracks, Abbey Road has contributions from all four of the Beatles, with George Harrison and Ringo Starr each providing their strongest entries ("Something" and "Here Comes the Sun" for Harrison, "Octopus's Garden" for Starr). Even the much-maligned "Maxwell's Silver Hammer" is far better than its reputation. The album has a blend of genres that work as a whole, serving as a snapshot of the band at the apex of their creativity.

The Beatles fell apart after recording Abbey Road, while Australian rockers AC/DC could have done the same after the death of original frontman Bon Scott, and even toyed with the idea of disbanding. Instead, they stayed together, with Scott's father encouraging them to do so. They recruited Brian Johnson and set out to record what would be the definitive AC/DC album: Back in Black. It was created as a tribute to Scott, with the all-black cover a sign of mourning, while simultaneously launching a new era for the band. That new era came with a commitment to elemental, arena-ready guitar riffs and straight-forward rock, with a frontman who brought his own energy instead of being a Scott-lite. To say it worked out pretty well is an understatement: Back in Black hit the top of the UK album chart, became the best-selling album that never reached number one in the US charts, and is currently the second best-selling album of all time in music history. There is legitimately not a single track that sucks, from opener "Hells Bells" (which, hopefully, aren't what you hear on the other side) to "Rock and Roll Ain't Noise Pollution."

These classic rock albums are masterpieces from beginning to end.

Another album that showcases the band's mastery over a wide range of genres is Led Zeppelin's Led Zeppelin IV. "Black Dog" and "Rock and Roll" are straight bangers, "Going to California" has an acoustic folk vibe, while "When the Levee Breaks" takes the original blues song by Kansas Joe McCoy and Memphis Minnie from 1929 and reinterprets it as a slow-groove, blues-rock masterpiece. Every member of the band is at their best, which comes through the album's most prominent track: "Stairway to Heaven." The delicate acoustic softness of its opening melds seamlessly into its hard-rock crescendo and a fiery solo from Jimmy Page that arguably stands as his finest, with Robert Plant owning the track with his uncanny vocal range. And hey, when you do say goodbye to this world, it's nice to know you don't need to waste your time finding the elevator to Heaven.

And if you make that trek to the top of the stairway, you'll find "Paradise City," which is also a track from one of rock's all-time greatest debut albums, Guns N' Roses' Appetite for Destruction. Neither the album or the band entered the music world quietly, but rather with a raw, explosive danger. They took the makeup, tights and hairspray that marked the hair-metal era and made it irrelevant overnight, ditching the sexualized, party-time dynamic in favor of a gritty, punk realism. The album balances radio-friendly(ish) tracks like "Sweet Child O' Mine" and "Welcome to the Jungle" with darker cuts like "Mr. Brownstone," making it an album of all killer, no filler. Why waste the time you have left listening to crap?

Knowing that this life is fleeting, you may want to get familiar with the fella that'll be hosting you for eternity after you've bought the farm: God. To that end, you'll want to throw on Stryper's game-changing 1986 album To Hell With the Devil. With it, the holy-roller band did the unimaginable by making Christian rock music actually good. Like, MTV good, where they were in heavy rotation with the likes of Mötley Crue and Poison. They had all the bombast and flash of their secular hair-metal peers, even better than most, with a message that was radically different. "Honestly" is as good a power ballad as you'll ever hear, while standout tracks "The Way" and "To Hell With the Devil" showcase the blistering fretwork of Oz Fox and vocalist Michael Sweet's uncanny pipes. In a word, To Hell With the Devil is heavenly.

As we learned from It's a Wonderful Life, every time a bell rings, an angel gets their wings. Assuming the bells you hear on side B of life aren't one of Hell's, then congratulations, you get your wings! But why wait until you take the big Adios, when you can pick up Aerosmith's second album, Get Your Wings. First, a little context: Aerosmith's self-titled debut in 1973 was not a success, unaided by Columbia's promotional efforts being diverted to tout Bruce Springsteen's debut album Greeting from Asbury Park, N.J. That, and as Tom Hamilton points out, "a lot of people took a look at us and said, 'They're like a Kmart version of the Stones.'" They were given one more chance: "Unless your next album is really, really good, you're not going to be recording artists anymore."

Suffice it to say, Get Your Wings was, is, really, really good. The biggest sin of Aerosmith was that it simply didn't sound like Aerosmith, and Get Your Wings reflected the band's emergence from the blues roots that dominated the first album into their own hard-rock identity. Joe Perry and Brad Whitford's dual-guitars were accentuated by producer Jack Douglas, who also helped frontman Steven Tyler hone his songwriting to reflect the now-iconic, swaggering persona he was developing as a frontman. The album would define the distinct Aerosmith sound from that point on, with tracks like "Same Old Song and Dance," "Seasons of Wither," "Train Kept a-Rollin,'" and the playful sleaze of "Lord of the Thighs" particular standouts.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Jason Voorhees Returns: Steve Miner To Direct 13th Entry Of ‘Friday The 13th,’ First Film In Franchise In Nearly Two Decades

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Lake dwellers, beware! Jason Vorhees, the machete-wielding masked murderer is back — set to return for the first theatrical feature in the iconic horror franchise Friday the 13th in nearly two decades.

Coming shortly on the heels of the Peacock/A24 series Crystal Lake, as well as the acclaimed Mike P. Nelson short Sweet Revenge, made to commemorate the 45th of Jason Universe, this entry in the franchise will be a big one, Lucky #13.

Steve Miner, who first put the machete in Jason’s hands in Friday the 13th Part II and introduced the legendary hockey mask in Friday the 13th Part III, is set to direct the 2027 film from a script penned by emerging screenwriter David Fliegel. Returning to the franchise’s independent roots, the film will be financed and produced by the Barsamian family, which controls the franchise through their company Horror, Inc. Related Stories News 'Crystal Lake's Jonathan Tison On Bringing Jason Into His Slasher Era, How He Found Out The Secretive Role He Landed: "I Was Shocked" Acquisitions Cineverse Wins North American Rights To Thriller-Horror 'The Night Driver' With Josh Lucas, Alyssa Milano & David Arquette

“For generations of fans, Jason’s identity is inseparable from the creative choices Steve Miner brought to the screen,” said Robbie Barsamian, Executive Vice President of Horror, Inc. “Reuniting with Steve under a fully independent model allows us the creative control to honor what makes Jason the greatest slasher of all time and deliver the definitive theatrical event that today’s audiences expect.” Watch on Deadline

Miner said, “When I put the hockey mask on Jason for Friday the 13th Part III, I knew it would create a truly scary image that would work for that movie. I had no idea that it would live on as one of the most iconic symbols in horror films. I’m thrilled to come back and continue the legacy.”

This will be the first Friday the 13th film since a 2009 reboot from Warner Bros. The next installment will welcome a new generation of horror fans into the Jason Universe while delivering the practical kills and terror longtime Friday the 13th fans crave.

CatchLight Studios — a production company behind horror films like A24’s Heretic and the Sandra Oh-led Umma — has been named as Horror, Inc.’s production partner, with Cineverse securing North America distribution rights, including theatrical.

Chris McGurk, Chairman and CEO of Cineverse, which has found major recent success with the Terrifier horror sequels, said: “Jason is one of the most recognizable figures in cinema history. We have a proven track record of empowering uncompromising genre filmmaking and connecting it directly with passionate audiences, and are honored to be a part of bringing Jason back to theaters where he belongs.”

Added Jeanette Volturno, Co-Founder of Catchlight Studios, “Bringing an icon like Jason back to the screen requires dedication to craft and a deep respect for the fandom. We’re excited to collaborate with Steve and Robbie to create a practically-shot film that is relentless, cinematic and terrifying.”

For more than 45 years, the billion-dollar Friday the 13th franchise has remained a defining force in global pop culture and genre filmmaking, known for its classic scares, iconic kills and multi-generational fan base that sets the standard for horror. The franchise launched with a namesake 1980 slasher, made independently for roughly $550,000 and acquired by Paramount in a bidding war, in which a group of counselors at Camp Crystal Lake, preparing to reopen the summer camp, are stalked and killed by an unseen assailant, while the memory of a boy who drowned there years earlier hangs over the grounds. The film, directed by Sean S. Cunningham, proved the foundation for a durable, low-budget sequel machine, with Warner Bros. and subsidiary New Line releasing some installments. While the original surprised with the revelation of (**SPOILER**) Pamela Vorhees as the assailant, later entries embraced Jason himself as the franchise’s terrifying driving force.

Today, the franchise is back in circulation through Crystal Lake, Peacock and A24’s prequel series, which premiered all eight episodes on Oct. 15. Featuring Linda Cardellini as matriarch Pamela Voorhees and Callum Vinson as a young Jason, the show generated 1.3 billion minutes viewed in its first 11 days alone, per Nielsen, bringing Peacock its fourth-best scripted-original debut. Past films in the series have collectively grossed nearly $450 million worldwide.

Over the decades since the original Friday the 13th, the franchise has also spawned video games, immersive experiences, comic books, collectibles, apparel and more. Horror, Inc. launched the Jason Universe to usher in a new era of expansion in the franchise in 2024.

News of the new Friday the 13th comes as a new installment in the equally popular A Nightmare on Elm Street franchise — revolving around Jason’s disfigured child murderer foe, Freddy Kreuger — percolates at Paramount Primal.

Miner is represented by David Gersh of The Gersh Agency. Fliegel is repped by Sukee Chew at Hopscotch Pictures and Ethan Neale at Paradigm.

Malaysian War Drama ‘The Ghost and the Gun’ Sets Global Netflix Release (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix will stream “The Ghost and the Gun” (“Peluru Senja“), a Bahasa Malaysia-language war drama set in Malaya in the years after World War II, around the world.

The film is the third feature from Indian filmmaker Anshul Tiwari, who is based in Singapore and previously directed “Before Life After Death” and “Bayaar.” Abhi Kumar wrote the screenplay.

The story unfolds in 1948. When British forces pull out of Malaya, two soldiers are left behind – one Malay, one British. The pair have to come to terms with their pasts in the war while defending a village targeted by armed rebels. Related Stories ‘White Tiger Column’ Directors on How Myanmar’s Resistance Speaks to Political Youth Movements in Brazil, Serbia and the U.S.: ‘Revolution Is on the Lips of a Lot of People’ Tony Award Winner Lois Smith Joins Colin West’s ‘Outside of Cleveland’: ‘She’s an Icon’ (EXCLUSIVE)

“While movies have explored World War II from every Western angle, the human cost paid across Malaya and Singapore remains largely unseen,” Tiwari said. “I was drawn to this story because the volatility of the Malayan Emergency feels painfully urgent today: the timeless tragedy of ordinary people caught in the crossfire of empires fighting for power.”

Malaysian actor Fabian Loo, an award winner known for “Rain Town” and “One Cent Thief,” heads the ensemble. He is joined by Hazman Al-Idrus, Asmara Abigail (“Impetigore,” “Stone Turtle”) and American actor Brett Tutor (“Monsters of Man”).

“Siti carries the weight of a whole region’s history in a very quiet way, and playing her in the Pahang dialect pushed me somewhere new,” Abigail said of her character. “I’m proud that such a Southeast Asian story can now reach audiences everywhere.”

The producers planned the film as an international collaboration from day one. Shooting took place at Viking Sunset Studios in Bali, where 14 sets were built to stand in for 1940s Malaya. The cinematographer came from India, a Malaysian team in Kuala Lumpur handled all of the film’s sound and color work, and a British composer wrote the score. Cast and crew members hailed from Malaysia, Indonesia, Singapore, India, Japan, the U.K. and the U.S.

“Bringing seven countries together on one production was never the easy route, but it was the right one,” said producer Debasmita Dasgupta of FilmsPositive. “Every department brought something of their own film culture to create a deeply rooted Southeast Asian story. What you see on screen is the result of seamless cross-border collaboration, proof that authentic regional stories have the power to command a global stage.”

Fellow producer Mikail Chowdhury of LDNO said films like this one rarely get made. “A Malay soldier in 1948 Malaya isn’t the story a traditional studio would back, but it’s one which audiences in this region have been waiting to see on screen and should be told,” Chowdhury said. “Independent film is where these stories survive and it is a privilege to have been part of it.”

Dasgupta and Chowdhury produce alongside Tim Willrich of Limbo Pictures and Bo H. Holmgreen, CEO of Viking Sunset Studios. Jessie White of Misfit Studios is executive producer with Mohsen Ashraf of Friendly Felix Studios, Tanveer Ali, Amreen Bashir of Amra Bangladeshi and Tahmid Khan of Auras.

White said Misfit enjoys supporting people who take creative chances on stories they believe in. “We’re proud to have helped make this film happen, and excited for audiences to discover it on Netflix,” White said.

After a nationwide cinema release in Malaysia and a smaller theatrical run in Indonesia, “The Ghost and the Gun” will launch on Netflix worldwide on Nov. 28.

Universal's Best Twilight / Harry Potter Replacement Has Been In Development Hell For Six Years

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Step aside, Twilight, and move over, Harry Potter; Universal has the perfect answer to both. It may seem an unlikely combination; Twilight is a "sparkly vampire" romance, while Harry Potter blazed the trail for the current generation of magic schools (complete with a healthy dose of a Chosen One destiny). And yet, have you ever noticed how the best original stories are a delightful mix of multiple ideas, creating something refreshingly new and yet delightfully familiar?

Step forward Tracy Wolff's Crave, which is a stunning blend of the two. It stars Grace Foster, a young orphan who winds up sent to what turns out to be a school for vampires, werewolves, dragons, witches, and more. And, of course, the different groups don't get on at all. The book is delightfully self-aware (at one point the vampire love-interest gives Grace a gift copy of Twilight), and it turns so many of the central tropes upside-down in a fantastic way.

Crave launched in 2020, and it's expanded into a full six-book series. Katmere Academy isn't Hogwarts; it's much more dangerous, a little closer to the dark academia books I've fallen in love with of late. The various factions initially feel almost like your traditional high school, where the jocks and the nerds all hang out together, but it soon becomes clear the racial "tribes" are much more dangerous. Grace has no idea what she's dealing with, and initially there's an entertaining sense of denial as readers figure out what's happening even as she tries to tell herself she's going nuts.

That said, my personal favorite twist here is that the heroes are not the Chosen Ones. In fact, this is a franchise where the Chosen One went wrong; where the vampire love interest's brother was believed to be a Chosen One, and was raised to believe he was something special. It's fascinating to realize how much this kind of philosophy can twist and scar a person, making the Crave story a fun interrogation of a trope we've seen played out so many times. It reminds me a little of Anakin Skywalker in the Star Wars prequels, but a little better done (with apologies to George Lucas).

As a lover of Twilight and Harry Potter, and someone who very much enjoys romantasy, I can honestly say Crave is a must-read. Wolff is wonderfully creative, and her world is so very immersive; even when she plays with familiar tropes and character beats, she does so with a nod and a wink. If you like vampires and werewolves, magic schools, or even dragon shifters, this is the series for you.

But here's the annoying thing; Universal acquired the rights to Crave before the first book even came out, with Universal’s SVP of Production Jeyun Munford and Creative Executive Christine Sun set to oversee the production. We've had zero news about the film since then, though, which is something of a surprise; unfortunately, Munford isn't even at Universal anymore, which may well mean this particular movie has been lost in development hell. If so, it's a real shame; at a time when both Harry Potter and Twilight are re-emerging, this is an epic that should really be given a chance to flourish.

For now, readers would be wise to pick up Crave for something that will definitely scratch that itch in the best possible way.

Pennsylvania Pebbles

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It hasn’t rained for weeks. That changes today, just in time to join forces with the Autumn leaves and northern Pennsylvania rocks to brew up a wicked hiker test.

They can’t help themselves, these white-tailed deer. They like to visit us campers. At 3:00AM this morning a deer, which had almost certainly sensed the rain coming, perhaps curious if there was space inside for it to keep dry. I told it that to save about $50 I bought a 1 person tent, it muttered a snort before prancing off.

The next thing I remember rain was pattering onto my tent. Shucks, I wasn’t going to escape with a dry tent this morning. I had been clever enough to tuck my groundsheet behind the rain fly so that the water would fall to the ground rather than pool up. The fact that I was camping on a layer of leaves was a boon as well, I wouldn’t have to deal with mud getting on everything. I fell back asleep but emerged from my tent shortly before sunrise to an eerie scene – the forest was enveloped in a thick mist. My tent looked sorry, the water weighing down the rain fly, the stakes no doubt shifting from their precarious posts during the night. Usually this is the time where I come up with some excuse to stay in my tent for half the morning, but not this time. The rain had brought about 10 degrees of warmth along with it, it was in the mid fifties. It wasn’t currently raining, so I certainly didn’t want to dilly dally only for it to start raining, making packing up a mess. I wolfed down my breakfast and set about packing. By 7:00AM I was making my way northbound.

I had made it a grand total on 1 mile north of the Leroy A. Smith shelter yesterday, leaving a little over 18 miles back to my van at the Delaware Water Gap. I knew rain could arrive at any time, there was a decent chance of it throughout the day. I was liking the foggy trail conditions, though it came at the expense of the valley views I had yesterday.

On the north side of Wind Gap a rocky yet mercifully short climb awaited me. Once the trail leveled out, I found that the area was still consumed with fog. As I continued my meandering, a small flock of birds, perhaps starlings, took off from the ground near the trail and disappeared into the white mist. Several chipmunks scampered into their burrows, revealing little more than a flash of brown fur to me. My thoughts ran to the interaction between these two very different species of animals. Where are the birds searching for food, potentially at the expense of the chipmunks? Had I stumbled upon nature’s version of capture the flag?

The scene on the trail early this morning.

I wouldn’t watch a scary movie before wandering into this part of the forest.

For a while the rain held off. I enjoyed more of the trail surrounded by fog from all sides. It creates some fascinating scenes for sure. I didn’t miss the valley views, I had something completely different to experience. Nature was about to throw something different to experience at me. Right after I turned onto the Wolf Rocks bypass trail, the mist that had been on and off all day turned into a light rain. I tossed on my poncho for the first time since Vermont. I had made excellent progress, most of the bad rocky sections were behind me. I trudged on through the fog and rain determined to stay on track for an early finish.

Suddenly I wasn’t alone, ahead of me a figure was descending the trail. On a day like this, it could only be a long distance hiker. A large yellow pack and sky blue sun hoodie confirmed as much. Then I realized that I knew those items and the face of the hiker well, Pebbles! I called out to her, yet again struck by the encounter as I was sure that this time she would have been far down the trail. In fact, she had spent Halloween trick-or-treating back in Unionville, NY. That explained why I only saw her once last week, jumping ahead on the trail should have put us on track to meet again the next day. She reported the town was very friendly to her and her thru-hiker costume. She may be a genius, I kicked myself for not thinking of the idea. Maybe I’ll buy some candy for myself at the post-halloween discount.

Pebbles and See You Tomorrow catch up on the trail.

I didn’t envy Pebbles, who was heading straight into the rocky sections that tested me yesterday. I, on the other hand, reached the gravel road near Mount Minsi. I could really make time on the wide open path. After a brief rocky descent on the AT I switched back onto the gravel road. I figured it would be a much smoother descent, and it also led straight back to the AT just before the trailhead where my van was parked. It was not perfect, there were plenty of rocks on the primitive road, but there were none of the step downs that I would have had on the AT. My ankle has taken a lot of abuse this year, but it feels up to finishing off the hike and I want to keep it that way. Just before the trailhead, I noticed and abrupt change in the color of the gravel on the road. It turned into a mix of turquoise and gray, a combination I felt was worth admiring for a few moments before finishing out my hike.

I was back at my van around 1:30PM and quickly got myself ready for town. The local chain cafe was open until 10:00PM giving me time to get all 4 blog posts from this week ready for publishing. With luck I could have another light Sunday coming up, just what I need as I enter the closing stages of my year long hike!

Yes, I caught myself admiring the rocks in northern Pennsylvania of all places!

The buyer pays the same price as they would otherwise, and your purchase helps to support The Trek's ongoing goal to serve you quality backpacking advice and information. Thanks for your support!

I’m See You Tomorrow (SYT), a 30-something hiker who loves hiking just for the thrill of seeing things that most people don’t. I have no problem eating the same things every day and, for me, over-analyzing everything is half the fun (sometimes more). I am new to backpacking but that won’t stop me from taking on an unorthodox hike of the Appalachian Trail in 2026. It’s not quite a yo-yo, but I have aspirations of hiking the entire trail twice in one year. For the rest of the story you’ll just have to start reading my blog!

9 Bedroom Makeover Tips for More Sex

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

And we’re starting with things you already have.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Your partner carries you into the bedroom à la your favorite #BookTok MMC, only for you both to remember the duvet is still covered with laundry that needs to be folded. Buzzkill. Or maybe you reach into your nightstand drawer for your wand vibrator, only to graze a stack of unpaid bills. Never mind…

“Your bedroom is already shaping how you feel about sex,” says Ashley Giddens, interior designer, artist and founder of Ashthetics NYC. It's not just clutter that can pull you out of the moment, or a tidy space that can help you stay in it, either, she says.

“Light impacts how you feel, which matters a lot when you're about to take your clothes off, while the textures impact what you feel beyond skin,” Giddens explains. Not to mention, the colors on the walls, sheets, and even the ceiling can influence how cozy, intimate, or downright sexy your bedroom feels, she says.

With that, a little bedroom makeover can help set the stage for more (and better) sex.

According to Philadelphia-based ASSECT-certified sex therapist and clinical licensed social worker Melanie Greenberg, LCSW, CST, MEd, Founder of Pulling Threads Therapy, changing your surroundings isn't a guaranteed libido booster. “But having a bedroom that is decorated and designed to make you comfortable, free of worry, relaxed, and connected to your senses and partner may make it easier to get in the mood,” she says.

Rest assured, you don't need a huge budget to bring it to life. Whether you've got $20 or $20,000 to spare, there's a way to make your bedroom a little sexier. Here are nine expert-approved places to start.

You might think a bedroom designed with sleep in mind and one designed for, well, doing it are two different beasts. But actually they are the same side of the coin: “If your bedroom doesn't help you sleep, you're probably not going to have much energy left for anything else,” Erica Corpening, co-founder and designer of Hidden Honey Homes, an adult-only boutique retreat emporium, tells Cosmopolitan.

On a physiological level, skimping on Zzz's can disrupt our endocrine system, potentially interfering with the hormones that support sexual functioning in the first place. That's why before you start shopping for silk sheets and sex swings, Corpening suggests that when designing a sex-friendly bedroom, “start with sleep.”

First and foremost, that means getting a comfortable mattress—whatever that means to you. In particular, however, take a beat to consider your preferred squishiness, how much the mattress squeaks when you move, and the weight limit. After all, if you can't get comfortable lying still, odds are you won't enjoy getting busy in missionary.

It also means considering the bed height you enjoy most. “A low platform frame can make the room feel grounded, relaxed, and a little more lounge-like,” says Corpening. It can also make getting into floor-based sex positions a whole lot easier. But it might not be accessible for people with hip or knee issues, or anyone who has difficulty getting up from low surfaces. On the flip side, a higher bed frame can give your room a more elevated, hotel-suite feel, while also making certain standing sex positions easier to pull off.

The lowest-cost suggestion on the list doubles as the most essential: Tidy up. “One of the sexiest things you can add to a bedroom is space,” says Corpening.

The debris of life (i.e., burp cloths, bills, yesterday's boxers) can keep your mind focused on everything on your to-do list—a serious turn-off (or sexual brake) for anyone whose desire is easily derailed by distraction. And without all those visual reminders of your responsibilities, your brain might finally have the bandwidth for something a little less responsible.

“So before you buy anything, clear off the nightstands and rescue the corner chair that's been holding last week's laundry,” says Corpening. And don't underestimate the wonders that under-the-bed storage, vacuum-sealed storage bags, and drawer organizers—all of which are just an Amazon order or Target run away—can do for your daily detritus (and, by extension, your sex life).

Materially speaking, what do you need on hand before, during, and after sex? Maybe you're a sensation seeker like yours truly and need a Wartenberg wheel, blindfold, or feather tickler to transition from your PG life to your X-rated one. Maybe you squirt, or are into golden showers, and like to lay down a water-resistant blanket before play. Or maybe you're a lube lover who would benefit from keeping a bottle within arm's reach rather than buried in the bathroom cabinet.

Whatever your pleasure preferences, take stock of your sex-specific supplies, then do what you can to make them as easy to access as possible.“Even small adjustments to a real setting can make a difference,” doctor of human sexuality Emily Nagoski, PhD, sex educator and author, writes in her 2024 book Come Together.

“It’s not super-sexy to have to get out of bed, dripping with fluids, and go into the bathroom to get a towel, especially on a cold New England night,” she writes. Her solution? Allocate a drawer, reachable from the bed, with towels dedicated to post-sex cleanup. “Some problems are just that easy to solve,” she writes.

Sex furniture, more or less, falls into two categories. First, there is the stuff that looks like, well, the kind of sex furniture that outfits a dungeon or BDSM playroom. I’m talking St. Andrew’s sex crosses, spanking benches, and sex horses. All of which, for the record, can be great bedroom makeover investments if they support your particular fantasies.

But there's also more discreet sex furniture, like sex pillows and wedges, which are primarily designed to support your bodies and make certain positions more comfortable. While unassuming, these seemingly simple additions can have a serious impact because “with better support, sex naturally improves,” Danny Griffin, the creative director of Liberator, tells Cosmopolitan.

“A firm, stable surface at the right angle takes the strain off knees, wrists, hips, and lower backs, so you can stay in a position long enough to actually enjoy it,” he says. Meanwhile, the few inches of lift provided by a pillow or wedge can significantly change how two bodies line up, he says, “which can mean deeper penetration or an easier path to the spot that matters, which is a big deal for couples with different heights or body types.”

Ultimately, whether you opt for a piece of furniture that screams sex dungeon or one that passes as a regular ‘ole cushion depends on your wants, needs, desires, and budget. To figure out what you should invest in, Corpening suggests, “Ask each other what you'd actually enjoy exploring, what sounds intriguing, playful, adventurous, or more comfortable. Then choose something that supports that experience.”

According to Giddens, another cheap change with a big payoff is a lighting revamp. “Bright light is inspection light, and it mostly puts you on display to yourself, which can make it hard to feel sensual,” she says. Bright lights have also been linked to feelings of unsafety, which is totally incompatible with good sex, she adds.

Low, warm light, on the other hand, can help create a sense of privacy and comfort, allowing you to “stop monitoring and start feeling,” she says. On top of that, warm lighting can soften skin texture and even out tone, she says, which may help you feel more confident in your birthday suit.

Your move: “Retire the overhead after dark, put two lamps low and close to the bed, and fill them with warm dimmable bulbs,” Giddens suggests. Specifically, look for bulbs rated 2700K or lower. “2200K gets close to candlelight,” she says.

And if you're going for more sex-party than spa-night vibes? “Swap one lamp to a pink or red bulb, or hang a small neon sign, and let that colored glow wash across one wall,” she suggests.

When choosing a color palette for your pleasure palace, take a cue from the bisexual Pride flag: “Pink is the sleeper pick,” Giddens tells Cosmopolitan. Associated with both calmness and intimacy, the hue can help create a more inviting atmosphere, she says. “It also sits in the same family as flushed cheeks and lips, so it tends to flatter skin,” she says.

“Red is the obvious answer, and it earns its reputation,” she says. On the walls, red can make a room feel smaller, “which in a bedroom reads as close and intimate,” she says.

For a moodier, more boudoir-like feel, consider deep jewel tones like plum and midnight navy, which make a room feel more enveloping, particularly when the lights are low, says Giddens. In fact, if you're tempted to go full black-walled for the ~drama~, she suggests using these colors instead. “They will give you the same moody depth with some warmth still in it, unless a little dungeon energy is exactly the goal,” she says.

Whatever shade you choose, don't commit based on how it looks at high noon. Instead, tape a few paint swatches beside your bed and see how they look under the lamps you actually use at night. “Warm bulbs can change paint a lot. Reds, pinks, and plums tend to get richer, while a cool blue or gray that looks crisp in daylight can go flat and muddy,” she says.

Before you rinse your brushes and fold the tarps, consider giving the ceiling a little love, too. “It's the move that almost nobody makes,” says Giddens. Yet “it's the surface you look at most when you're lying in bed, it's usually still builder white, and there's no furniture to work around, so a gallon of paint and a weekend afternoon gets you a completely different room.” Cheers to efficiency!

Of course, the color you use on the ceiling matters. “A ceiling bounces that color back down onto your skin,” she says. “My one hard rule is no green on the ceiling, because that same reflection casts green onto faces and bodies, which is about as unsexy as color gets.”

A saturated pink or a warm peach above the bed, on the other hand, can cast a warm, flattering glow on the naked bodies below. Red, meanwhile, is better left to the walls or smaller accents, as Giddens says it can feel heavy and intrusive overhead.

Good news: If the thought of spending your weekend in coveralls with a paint roller makes you want to crawl under the covers (to sleep), you can still join the color play. Same goes if your landlord has a strict No-Paint-Allowed policy. “If paint is off the table entirely, change your sheets,” suggests Giddens.

“Sheets are the biggest piece of color that sits right next to your skin, and fabric reflects color the same way a ceiling does,” she says. As such, she suggests picking them the way you'd choose a makeup shade. “Pink, peach, deep red, and plum bounce warmth back onto your face and body, while gray-green does the opposite.”

A well-placed sex mirror can turn an otherwise innocent situation downright indecent faster than a well-timed 'good girl' can make me forget my own name (just me?).

Sure, there's the tried-and-true mirror-above-the-bed approach. Though, word to the wise: Don't leave this one to any ol' Taskrabbit. A broken mirror might mean seven years of bad luck, but a broken mirror above your bed could mean a trip to the ER. So make sure yours is professionally and securely installed.

But you have other options, too. “If you place a mirror across from a chaise, or beside a doorway, play can spill into other parts of the space,” says Corpening. “You might catch a bare shoulder in your peripheral vision, or the two of you moving together in the glass, and then it's gone. It can heighten your awareness of your body and build anticipation without anyone saying a word,” she says.

Meanwhile, a mirror near a window can serve double duty. “If you hang it near a window, it bounces daylight around and makes the room feel open during the day,” says Corpening. A win for afternoon nookie fans, indeed.

Star Wars 50th Anniversary Release Will Include New Music from John Williams

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Paired with a fancy new ‘celebratory’ logo

Star Wars is celebrating its 50th anniversary in 2027. The original cut of the first film (later subtitled Episode IV – A New Hope) will hit theaters in February, and it will be accompanied by brief new music from longtime Star Wars composer John Williams.

The theatrical re-release will add a new logo celebrating the 50th anniversary, accompanied by a score orchestrated by Williams. He orchestrated the music for all nine Skywalker Saga films, helping the series craft an indelible identity. The franchise's music has expanded as other composers have brought their sonic vision to Star Wars, like Ludwig Göransson and his award-winning score for The Mandalorian.

You can hear Williams' new score for the logo in theaters when Star Wars returns Feb. 19, 2027. It will be shown in various formats, including 70 mm IMAX. That means you'll be able to see Han definitely shoot first on the biggest and best screens available.

Williams' Star Wars score will get the spotlight in April 2027 with Star Wars in Concert: The Skywalker Saga at the Hollywood Bowl. C-3PO himself, Anthony Daniels, will host orchestral performances of Williams' scores from all nine Skywalker Saga films.

"We look forward to celebrating with our fans all year long — from the releases of Ahsoka and Star Wars: Starfighter to exclusive merchandise, new publishing stories, unforgettable experiences and interactive games. We are so thankful for our amazing community and hope these experiences bring joy to Star Wars fans everywhere," Lucasfilm co-president Lynwen Brennan said in a news release.

As Brennan mentioned, a new season of Ahsoka will debut on Disney Plus next year while the Ryan Gosling-led Starfighter will hit theaters on May 28, 2027. Disney will also broadcast a Star Wars Super Bowl, release retro toys, stream a documentary The Hero Awakens on Disney Plus, and more to celebrate Star Wars throughout 2027.

From the mind behind, ‘Do or do not. There is no try.’

Eminem Releases ‘Infinite’ EP on Streaming & 30th Anniversary Documentary Surrounding His Debut Album

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

New mixes and additional production have been added to the project, which will be sold as physicals as well.

Long before the days of meeting Dr. Dre, Eminem was inspired by the likes of 2Pac and Nas when recording his debut album, Infinite, which arrived exactly 30 years ago on Thursday (Nov. 12).

Em celebrated the 30th anniversary of his independent release by bringing an EP version of the project to streaming services for the first time, which features six of the LP’s 11 songs: “Infinite,” “It’s O.K.,” “Tonite,” “313,” “Open Mic” and “Never 2 Far.”

Physical editions of Infinite are available at Org Music, with vinyl, CD and cassette options available for pre-order; they will arrive Feb. 26. Jake Bass and Mike “Slim” Wilde provide new mixes and additional production to the project.

Org Music also released a 10-minute documentary celebrating Infinite‘s anniversary, featuring interviews with key players such as the Bass Brothers as well as Eminem, and archival footage from the mid-’90s era surrounding the project. (Watch the short film above.)

“After the Infinite release, we ended up [giving] the rest of the CDs and cassettes that didn’t sell just going to the places we could give ’em out,” Em recalls in the minidoc after selling less than 100 copies in ’96.

Released by WEB Entertainment, the album’s 1996 arrival didn’t bring Eminem the commercial breakthrough and radio success he had originally hoped for. Mark and Jeff of the Bass Brothers took Em under their wing and invested in the Detroit rapper, allowing him and other future members of D12 to record at their Bassmint studio. Mark saw the vision for Eminem, while Jeff just didn’t get it at the time. Infinite‘s production was helmed by Em and Denaun Porter.

However, it all began to change when Em stopped making music seeking validation and dropped his filter to lean into his Slim Shady alter ego heading into 1997’s The Slim Shady EP, which caught the attention of Dr. Dre.

“As soon as I stopped giving a f — k about what I was saying, people started giving a f — k. It was like a reverse effect,” Eminem says in the short film.

The rapper’s mainstream explosion came with The Slim Shady LP in February 1999, which debuted at No. 2 on the Billboard 200, while the Detroit native picked up his first top 40 hit on the Billboard Hot 100 with “My Name Is.”
