Casting Carl In The Dungeon Crawler Carl TV Series: 9 Perfect Actors

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Peacock’s upcoming live-action Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show is bringing Matt Dinniman’s literary sensation to screens, and the hype is palpable. The books’ combination of outrageous violence, devastating emotional twists and turns, and gloriously ridiculous videogame-inspired dungeon mechanics have always seemed perfect for a live-action series, and now it’s finally happening. Naturally, the biggest questions so far from the DCC fandom concern casting, especially when it comes to who will play Carl.

Peacock has already confirmed that audiobook narrator Jeff Hays will voice Princess Donut, but Carl remains uncast. It’s not an easy role to fill, either. Physicality is important, but any actor playing Carl in the Dungeon Crawler Carl series needs more than the right build. Balancing comedy with authenticity in Carl’s exhausted sarcasm, practical problem-solving, loyalty to his companions, and steadily intensifying anger are equally essential. Fortunately, several actors feel perfectly equipped to fill the toe rings, leather jacket, and heart-covered boxer shorts of the titular dungeon crawler.

There’s something fitting about casting a professional wrestler as a man forced to survive by entertaining an audience. Cody Rhodes understands how physical punishment, personal grudges, and carefully managed spectacle can turn a character in a combat-heavy story arc into someone a crowd gets emotionally invested in. Beyond the wrestling ring, his performance as Guile in the 2026 Street Fighter movie proves that his comic delivery and willingness to embrace an exaggerated character translates perfectly to scripted movies and TV.

One of Carl’s subtler but most important qualities is a complete lack of embarrassment: the actor wearing those boxer shorts can’t seem self-aware about how ridiculous they look. Cody Rhodes did exactly this with Guile’s absurd hair in Street Fighter. Rhodes would also bring an interesting quality to Carl’s relationship with his growing fanbase. Carl increasingly understands the value of being watched, even as he despises the system doing the watching. A WWE superstar has direct experience building audience allegiance, meaning Cody Rhodes could make this important aspect of Carl’s journey feel totally believable.

A huge man accompanied by a smaller, sharper-tongued companion is already familiar territory for Peter Claffey. As Ser Duncan the Tall in the Game of Thrones spinoff A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms, Claffey gives an intimidating figure a mix of being approachable and comedically awkward that makes him worth supporting. The gap between how formidable Dunk looks and how uncertain he feels would be perfect for Carl in the Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show, especially during the early dungeon floors before he and Donut have a grasp of the gaming mechanics that now define their world.

Peter Claffey manages to make kindness compelling, and this would also be essential. Carl’s willingness to help other crawlers matters because it repeatedly creates complications, forcing him to risk his own safety and plans to advance through the dungeon. He could convincingly sell the frustrated pause before Carl accepts that he can’t leave someone behind without the moment coming across as sanctimonious. What’s more, his ability to conjure humble sincerity would make him an excellent counterpart to Donut. His ability to remain emotionally present while being verbally outmaneuvered could bring everything endearing about their partnership in the books to the screen for Peacocks’ DCC series.

Alan Ritchson is a clearly suitable choice to play Carl in the Dungeon Crawler Carl series, and not just because of his size. His work as Jack Reacher in Reacher demonstrates how effectively he can carry action scenes filled with spontaneous, explosive violence that also require a character to think on their feet. Carl needs an actor who can deliver that combination. Ritchson can make a character look as though he is assessing a room even while everyone else assumes he is simply preparing to punch something.

However, Carl needs a comedic talent as well as an actor experienced with action. Fortunately, Alan Ritchson also fits the bill here. His performance as Thad Castle in Blue Mountain State, for example, shows that Ritchson is comfortable with absurdity, indignity, and enormous reactions, all useful qualities in a dungeon where humiliating achievements can interrupt horrific events. Most crucially, though, Carl’s confidence must feel earned through the weight of surviving terrible experiences. Combining Ritchson’s comic vulnerability with his action presence could achieve exactly that.

Carl’s voice is one of the most important casting considerations for Peacock’s Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show, and few actors feel primed to tick this box like Jensen Ackles. His gravelly delivery could carry Carl’s irritation and skepticism about his and Donut’s new reality observations with ease. His years as Dean Winchester in Supernatural demonstrate how effectively Ackles can connect sarcasm with exhaustion, grief, and fierce protectiveness. He’s an actor who knows how to make hilarious dialogue keep its comedic value without it seeming like the character is deliberately trying to crack a joke, which is what Carl needs.

Dean Winchester isn’t the only Jensen Ackles role that makes him a great candidate for the Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show cast, either. Soldier Boy in The Boys gave Ackles opportunities to combine comic timing with genuine menace. This would be essential for Carl, who regularly pivots from mild irritation to dangerous rage once his patience finally disappears. While he may be a little older than Carl is in the books (Carl is around 27 whereas Ackles, at time of writing, is 48), he could make DCC’s central dungeon crawler convincingly feel like someone whose habit of swallowing pain has finally reached its limit.

Charisma is just as important for surviving as violence is in Dungeon Crawler Carl, which makes Glen Powell an especially interesting choice to play Carl in the cast of the Peacock show. Carl does not enter the dungeon with any experience as an entertainer, but he gradually learns that leaning into his newfound intergalactic celebrity can keep him alive. Powell could make that development convincing without presenting Carl as someone who’d normally seek fame. His performance as Gary Johnson in Hit Man is the perfect example here, as it demonstrates an ability to move between unassuming awkwardness and deliberately constructed confidence. That range would be a solid foundation for Carl’s increasingly public rebellion as the DCC TV series unfolds.

Glen Powell’s suitability also extends beyond Carl’s talk show appearances. He’s got a handle on the action genre too, as proved by movies like The Running Man and Top Gun: Maverick. Any DCC Carl actor also needs to capture the character’s flashes of satisfaction when an outrageous plan works, which is something Powell has already done when playing storm chaser Tyler Owen in Twisters. Finally, as far as comedy goes, Powell has considerable experience in the genre with projects like How To Make A Killing and Chad Powers. All in all, he has a range of experience that, when brought together, make him stand out as a well-rounded option to lead the Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show cast.

Surviving the world dungeon takes more than strength. It takes amazing psychological endurance too, and Jack O’Connell excels at playing men who keep going when they have every reason to collapse. In ’71, his performance as Gary Hook makes desperation the driving force of survival and the spark that powers every moment of resourceful ingenuity. He could bring that same urgency to Carl’s struggles, especially on the lower floors when every encounter requires quick decision-making in the face of what feels like guaranteed death. O’Connell would make those victories look costly, ensuring Carl remains a vulnerable person even as his abilities become more spectacular.

Roles like James Cook in Skins and Jimmy in 28 Years Later: The Bone Temple also lend themselves to a potential Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show casting. O’Connell can make it clear that reckless confrontational behavior is a front for deep hurt and anger beneath the bravado. These are qualities that would suit Carl’s increasingly personal war against the dungeon’s operators as the DCC series reached later seasons. He may not have the physicality many readers associate with Carl, but he definitely has the ability to bring forth the emotional depth that makes him such an endearing character.

Casting horror icon Bill Skarsgård as Carl in Dungeon Crawler Carl may seem like a left field choice at first, but that’s only until one has seen Boy Kills World. As the titular Boy in the 2023 action thriller, Skarsgård throws himself into a world of extravagant videogame-inspired brutality, grotesque entertainment, and deliberately ridiculous action. His work on the movie is all the proof needed that he’d be perfect for Carl’s increasingly punishing battles. However, it’s not just his action experience that makes him a surprising yet suitable option.

A solid character actor, Bill Skarsgård has proved in various roles that he can convincingly bring forward a character’s inner suffering while participating in something spectacularly absurd. That balance is essential to an adaptation where the audience should laugh without losing sight of what survival costs. He could take the physical performance of Boy Kills World and bring it to what makes the likes of his work in IT and Nosferatu iconic. Yes, Pennywise and Count Orlok are wildly different characters to Carl, but they still prove Skarsgård understands how to completely transform his vocal delivery and physical presence for a character, which any good choice for an actor playing Carl needs to.

Being the biggest person in the room doesn’t mean being the most confident and self-assured, and Tom Hopper has considerable experience exploring that gap. His performance as Luther Hargreeves in The Umbrella Academy required him to play a physical powerhouse riddled with insecurity, wounded pride, and a fear that he’s never going to be the savior the world clearly needs. That mixture is exactly what would make Hopper’s performance as Carl tonally ideal. The world dungeon constantly forces Carl into humiliating situations and inflicts devastating losses that cut any celebration of victory short, and Hopper could make those indignities funny while preserving the sense that a real person is enduring them.

Tom Hopper in the DCC TV series would mean the show had a lead who could retain the openness that makes Carl approachable while also capturing the suspicion and practical ruthlessness that surviving the dungeon demands. Even more crucially, though, Umbrella Academy’s Luther is also a character defined by his compassion for his found family. This is also true of Carl. Carl’s affection for Donut should never feel like a subplot. It influences his decisions, establishes his priorities, and keeps the escalating spectacle emotionally grounded. Hopper could make caring for her seem completely natural, even during their most ridiculous arguments.

Austin Butler has already played a man whose decision to look after a cat pulls him into a violent nightmare, because that’s pretty much the premise of the 2025 movie Caught Stealing. However, his performance as Hank Thompson provides a much stronger reason to consider him for Carl beyond both characters regularly risking their lives for a cat. In the film, Butler made an increasingly battered protagonist convincingly overcome incredibly dangerous odds without relying on effortless competence. Hank is an ordinary guy caught up in events that keep becoming more and more violent, which is the core foundation of Carl as a character.

Hank in Caught Stealing isn’t the only role that singles out Austin Butler for the Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show, either. As Feyd-Rautha Harkonnen in Dune: Part Two, he demonstrates that he can be physically menacing, and as the lead in Elvis he captures the struggles of balancing celebrity with one’s moral compass. All in all, Butler has a slew of appropriate experience that would lend itself well to playing Carl in the Dungeon Crawler Carl TV show, and when it comes to proven range, he’s among the best choices available.

Santa Barbara Film Festival Awards: Anne Hathaway Set For American Riviera Honor

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

UPDATED, 10 AM: Anne Hathaway is set to receive the Santa Barbara Film Festival‘s 2027 American Riviera Award, which recognizes actors who have made a significant contribution to American cinema.

She will sit for an in-person tribute and conversation on February 5 that celebrates her career, with a focus on her performance as Penelope in Christopher Nolan’s billion-dollar-grossing epic The Odyssey. She also starred alongside Meryl Streep in this year’s long-awaited sequel The Devil Wears Prada 2.

“Anne Hathaway has always impressed me with her theatrical sophistication, unwavering commitment and remarkable versatility as a performer,” said SBIFF Executive Director Roger Durling. “In The Odyssey, she delivers a breathtaking performance, one that stands among the finest of her career, channeling simmering rage and profound emotional complexity into a portrayal that is at once fierce, vulnerable, poignant and unforgettable.”

Previous recipients of the fest’s American Riviera Award include Ethan Hawke, Zoe Saldaña, Mark Ruffalo, Brendan Fraser, Kristen Stewart, Delroy Lindo, Renée Zellweger, Viggo Mortensen, Sam Rockwell, Jeff Bridges, Michael Keaton, Rachel McAdams, Patricia Arquette, Robert Redford, Quentin Tarantino, Martin Scorsese, Annette Bening, Sandra Bullock, Mickey Rourke, Tommy Lee Jones, Forrest Whitaker, Philip Seymour Hoffman, Kevin Bacon and Diane Lane. Watch on Deadline

Read on to see the other 2027 SBIFF awards recipients who have been announced.

PREVIOUSLY, October 30: The Santa Barbara Film Festival on Friday named the recipients of its 2027 Virtuoso Awards. The honorees and their films are Carrie Coon (The Liberation), Mariana di Girolamo (Wild Horse Nine), Rosalind Eleazar (Misty Green), Jordan Firstman (Club Kid), Anthony Ippolito (I Play Rocky), Jaafar Jackson (Michael), Inde Navarrette (Obsession) and Kate O’Flynn (Tender Loving Care).

Per SBIFF, the Virtuoso Award recognizes a select group of talent whose exceptional work in films has placed them at the center of the conversation around the year’s most noteworthy cinematic performances.

“This year’s Virtuosos are a powerhouse group from the most talked-about films of the season. I think it’s especially fitting that so many of them are international performers and I can’t wait to help welcome them all to Santa Barbara,” said Karger.

The 42nd Santa Barbara International Film Festival is set for February 3-13 in the coastal California city. See below for the fest’s other 2027 awards winners revealed so far.

PREVIOUSLY, September 24: John Malkovich, a two-time Oscar nominee who won the Best Actor prize at this year’s Venice Film Festival for his work in Martin McDonagh’s upcoming Wild Horse Nine, has been selected to receive the Santa Barbara Film Festival’s Outstanding Performer of the Year Award.

The honor will be bestowed during the 42nd annual SBIFF, which runs February 3-13, 2027.

Malkovich leads off the awards announcements from the fest, which has become a valued stop on the movie-awards circuit. Last year, Michael B. Jordan won the same award ahead of his eventual Best Actor Oscar win for Sinners.

Other SBIFF honorees in the category include Ralph Fiennes, Bradley Cooper, Cate Blanchett, Will Smith, Aunjanue Ellis, Sacha Baron Cohen, Adam Driver, Rami Malek, Margot Robbie and Allison Janney, Ryan Gosling and Emma Stone, Brie Larson and Saoirse Ronan, Steve Carell, Cate Blanchett, Jennifer Lawrence, Viola Davis, James Franco, Colin Firth, Penélope Cruz, Angelina Jolie, Helen Mirren, Heath Ledger, Kate Winslet and Charlize Theron.

In the black comedy Wild Horse Nine, Malkovich stars with Sam Rockwell as a pair of CIA agents who travel from Santiago to Easter Island on the eve of the 1973 Chilean coup, confronting their dark pasts and becoming entangled in present-day conspiracies. The Searchlight Pictures pic hits theaters November 6.

“A legendary actor gives this year in Wild Horse Nine his best performance to date in which he combines pathos, volatility, vulnerability and tenderness,” SBIFF executive director Roger Durling said Thursday in a release announcing the award. “It’s a high-wire act that shows us what a commanding artist he is, and we are thrilled to be honoring him with our Outstanding Performer of the Year Award.”

Karla Souza To Star In ‘Grey’s Anatomy’ Texas Spinoff

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Karla Souza (How to Get Away with Murder, 56 Days) has been cast as a lead in ABC‘s new Grey’s Anatomy spinoff set in West Texas. She is the first actor to join the untitled 20th Television studio series from executive producers Shonda Rhimes, Betsy Beers, Meg Marinis, and Ellen Pompeo.

Created by Grey’s Anatomy creator Rhimes and current showrunner Marinis, the new show is described as an edgy drama about a team at a rural West Texas medical center — the last chance for care before miles of nowhere.

Souza will play Carolina “Lina” Hall, a haunted and cynical doctor in her 40s who runs a medical center in a small town in West Texas. Related Stories News 'Dancing With The Stars' Recap: Dance Offs On Grammy Night Bring Tough Competition To Quarterfinals News Luke Bryan, Megan Moroney & Vince Gill Among Performers Added To CMA Awards

The project reunites Souza with Rhimes and Beers after the actress starred in the hit ABC series How to Get Away with Murder, executive produced by the duo via Shondaland. Watch on Deadline

As Deadline reported last week, the untitled new Grey’s Anatomy spinoff will be the first series to shoot outside of Los Angeles in the franchise’s 23-year history. Unable to secure a California tax credit, the offshoot, currently in pre-production, now calls Albuquerque, New Mexico home. Production is expected to begin in early 2027, around February/March, sources tell Deadline, for a premiere during the 2027-28 season.

Shondaland has a lengthy history of shooting in California, including the Seattle-set Grey’s Anatomy and its previous spinoffs, Private Practice and Station 19, as well as two series set in Washington, D.C., ABC’s Scandal and Netflix’s The Residence, the Philadelphia-set ABC drama How to Get Away With Murder, and the network’s The Catch.

Souza is perhaps best known for playing Laurel opposite Viola Davis in ABC’s How to Get Away with Murder. Most recently, she starred as a series regular in Prime Video’s 56 Days opposite Dove Cameron and Avan Jogia, and prior to that, she played Marina on ABC’s Home Economics and starred opposite Jamie Foxx in Netflix’s Day Shift. She also recently received two International Emmy Awards for producing and starring in Dive (aka La Caida) for Prime Video. Souza is repped by Gersh, Untitled and Gang Tyre.

Chiefs will stick with Harrison Butker as their kicker

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Harrison Butker keeps missing field goals, but the Chiefs are not ready to make a change at kicker.

Chiefs head coach Andy Reid said at a Wednesday press conference that the team will stick with Butker for this Sunday’s game in Atlanta.

Butker missed a 56-yard field goal against the Jets last Sunday and then missed again from 51 yards after the Jets were flagged for being offside on the initial kick. He’s missed field goals in each of the last three games and is 12-of-19 on field goals over the entire season.

Butker’s shaky performance led the Chiefs to sign Justin Tucker to the practice squad late last month, but neither his presence nor Butker’s continued struggles has prompted Reid to make a change.

Marvel Officially Releases New Avengers Movie on Disney+ in 48 Hours

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Hannah covers horror, sci-fi, and games at Collider, where she writes news and features, reviews new releases, and interviews the people who make them. She’s helping build out Collider’s gaming coverage, with Pokémon as one of her specialties. If she isn’t writing about a monster, she’s probably watching one on a tape from her VHS collection.

When the Avengers assembled in 2019, the big question was who would make it out of Endgame. Seven years later, one of the biggest questions about the next Avengers movie is what to make of a familiar face. Robert Downey Jr. is back as Doctor Doom, and the directors who gave Tony Stark his goodbye are the ones bringing him back to Marvel in a much different role.

Anthony and Joe Russo have already gone back to Endgame once this year. The September rerelease opened with an estimated $86 million worldwide, giving Marvel a chance to put its next Avengers movie in front of people who’d paid to watch the last one again. Anthony Russo said the original was made to finish several characters’ stories. When the brothers started working out what Doomsday would be, they realized they needed to pick up where they’d left off.

Marvel Studios has released a new “Countdown to ‘Doomsday’” TV spot and poster ahead of Avengers Endgame: Encore’s Disney+ debut on Friday, November 13. The updated cut runs about four minutes longer than the 2019 movie and includes revised color grading and scenes that connect it to Avengers: Doomsday. The spot is available to watch now, a couple of days ahead of the extended cut’s streaming debut.

Four villains, and four arguments the films were honest enough to actually let them make. Here is the one you lean towards, the part of it that holds up, and the exact point where it stops holding up.

You align with the one who was right about the problem and wrong about the solution. Killmonger is the most persuasive villain Marvel has produced because the film never pretends his anger came from nowhere.

You align with the protector, which is the most understandable and most dangerous position on this list. Namor is not chasing power or revenge, he is doing whatever keeps his people hidden, and he has been doing it for centuries.

You align with the patient one, the only man on this list with no powers, no army and no hurry. Zemo lost his family in Sokovia and then spent years working out that you do not fight the Avengers, you introduce them to each other's secrets.

You align with the one who decided the answer was arithmetic. Thanos is not angry and not grieving in any way he will admit to, he simply reached a conclusion and never once revisited it, which is precisely what makes him frightening.

Disney+ has a collection for anyone planning a rewatch before December, along with a five-episode companion podcast hosted by Iman Vellani, who plays Ms. Marvel, and Frank Alvarez. Each episode revisits an individual movie from the Infinity Saga and discusses what might still matter for the next Avengers movie. The last episode covers Endgame and dropped alongside the theatrical rerelease.

Going through another rewatch of the Infinity Saga is finally getting some serious payoff soon, though. Endgame was about what the Avengers were willing to do to get their own world back, while Doomsday brings a magnifying glass to the multiversal consequences of such decisions. Marvel's next theatrical release brings heroes from three universes into the same fight, including the Fantastic Four and the Fox-era X-Men. The majority of those characters, of course, have never had to deal with the Avengers or the choices they made, giving the Russos a reason to go back to the end of the Infinity Saga before asking what happens when those worlds collide.

Avengers Endgame: Encore debuts Friday, November 13, exclusively on Disney+. Avengers: Doomsday opens in theaters December 18.

Todd Monken: I intend for Deshaun Watson being our starting QB

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Browns head coach Todd Monken opened his Wednesday press conference by confirming that Deshaun Watson will be back as the team’s starting quarterback for their Week 10 game against the Texans.

Watson did not play last week after missing a pair of practices while in the concussion protocol and the team’s plans for this Sunday remained a topic of conversation because Monken declined to say who would start during a Monday press conference. Monken explained that response by saying that he wanted to talk to the team’s coaching staff before adding that he also didn’t feel a need to make a public proclamation.

“Ultimately, the full body of work, the previous eight games we had played,” Monken said. “Deshaun played pretty well. That was my intention, I just wanted to make sure to run it by my staff before I said it publicly. I don’t know why I have to say it publicly.”

Monken was asked if he will be making a quarterback determination on a weekly basis moving forward.

“I don’t look at it week-to-week and I don’t look at it as long term,” Monken said. “We’re judged each week. I am, he is, our team is. . . . I anticipate him being our starter. That’s what he’s been, he’s been our starter. . . . I have no intention of Deshaun not being our starter.”

The Texans are 5-4 heading into their game with the Texans and they have a bye in Week 11. If they are unable to stay on the right side of .500 after that break, Monken will likely field more questions about the plan for Watson moving forward.

Rex Ryan is arrested after allegedly running over feet of pedestrians with his truck

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

No, it’s not The Onion. No, it’s not April Fool’s Day.

Former Jets and Bills coach and current (for now) ESPN analyst Rex Ryan has been arrested in Nashville after allegedly running over the feet of two pedestrians with his truck.

Via Edward Lewis of the New York Post, the Metropolitan Nashville Police Department said that Ryan turned himself in “on six outstanding misdemeanor warrants” for “striking two pedestrians with his pickup truck” just on November 5.

“The pedestrians were crossing Broadway from 4th Avenue with a walk sign when the pickup truck turned right from 4th Avenue running over both victims’ feet and making contact with one of the individual’s legs,” the Metropolitan Nashville Police Department said.

“The pedestrians said the vehicle kept going, but stopped for the red light at Broadway and Representative John Lewis Way where they waved down the driver. They said he stepped out of the truck to argue with them, got back in, and drove away. They took pictures of the truck’s license plate and the driver.”

Ryan faces two counts of leaving the scene of an accident, two counts of failure to give information/render aid, and two counts of failure to obey a traffic control signal.

Ryan became notorious in 2010 for a foot-fetish video that appeared online. He made light of the situation last month on ESPN, saying “I’m an expert in a few things. One of them is feet.”

DC Studios’ Latest Reboot: James Gunn and Peter Safran’s Exit Is About Cost-Cutting, Not Just Creative Issues

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

When they were hired to run DC Studios, James Gunn and Peter Safran were tasked with turning the struggling comic book company into a true rival to Marvel. Four years later, the two have been handed their walking papers before they were able to accomplish their task.

Skydance, which recently bought Warner Bros. Discovery and with it the rights to heroes like Superman, Batman, and the other DC staples that Gunn and Safran were trying to reinvigorate, opted to go in another, cheaper direction. They brought in Jeremy Latcham, a low-profile executive who, like Gunn, had spent years working for Marvel, to oversee DC’s latest reboot. Related Stories ‘Paper Tiger’ Review: Adam Driver Gives a Career-Best Performance in James Gray’s Devastating Tragedy ‘Iron Boy’ Trailer: Beloved Animated Feature Oscar Contender Throws a Whole Watercolor World Out of Whack

There have been mutterings that the change was because of the commercial performance of DC’s films, and the track record has been a mixed bag. “Superman” did what it needed to do, earning $618.7 million worldwide on a $225 million budget, but it wasn’t a blockbuster on the level of Christopher Nolan’s “Dark Knight” films or “Aquaman.”

But “Supergirl” was an unmitigated disaster, earning $126.5 million against a $170 million budget, resulting in losses of roughly $100 million. And “Clayface,” a body horror film produced for $44 million, has been a modest success, earning approximately $110 million. So no, Gunn and Safran had yet to produce a “Guardians of the Galaxy”-style smash that taps the zeitgeist. Though, to be fair, Marvel hasn’t made as many of those lately either. Tastes have changed, and comic book movies, at least ones not starring Spider-Man, aren’t as popular as they once were.

But look closer, and Gunn and Safran’s ouster is about something more than their films’ commercial performances. It’s about cost-cutting too, something that Skydance, with its $80 billion in net debt, needs to start chipping away at if it’s going to realize the $6 billion in savings it’s promised to find.

The duo’s deal, which was set to expire in 2027, was unusually generous. Safran not only got paid to run the comic book label, he was also allowed to run The Safran Company, the wildly successful production company behind “The Conjuring” franchise (former Netflix film chief Dan Lin, in contrast, divested from his company Rideback when he went over to the streamer). Then there was Gunn, who commanded fees for producing films like “Supergirl” and “Clayface,” as well as fees for those DC films that he wrote and directed, such as “Superman” and “Man of Tomorrow.” Gunn’s total haul for a DC film could be as high as $10 million for all his various duties, according to insiders.

When Warner Bros. Discovery chief David Zaslav offered the pair such a sweet deal, comic book movies looked more durable and he needed to make a splash after DC produced flops like “Aquaman and the Lost Kingdom” and “The Flash.” To sweeten the offer, Zaslav also allowed Gunn and Safran to be relatively autonomous, reporting directly to him, whereas Skydance wanted the DC leadership to be under Skydance Motion Picture Group chiefs Dana Goldberg and Josh Greenstein.

Skydance has also been stunned by the financial picture of Warner Bros.’ film unit since taking over. The studio has suffered a devastating wave of flops this year, losing hundreds of millions on a slate that included bombs such as “Digger,” “The Bride!,” and last weekend’s “The Cat in the Hat.” One insider put the estimated losses at nearing $1 billion. Of course, most of those films were greenlit under Michael De Luca and Pam Abdy, who were let go as soon as David Ellison got the keys to the Warner lot, and have nothing to do with Gunn or Safran. But it does put pressure on Skydance to turn things around and improve the film division’s margins, fast. On an earnings call this week, Ellison acknowledged the film division faces “headwinds” and “has underperformed this year.” He went on to pledge to “apply the same playbook we did at Paramount: greenlighting more selectively.”

Safran remains widely respected on the lot for his stewardship of DC, and is praised for trying to keep budgets in line and for cleaning up the company’s byzantine production practices so that its films, shows, and comic books had a unifying creative voice. Gunn, though a gifted filmmaker, had a harder time adjusting to life in the c-suite, something he admitted to this week in a lengthy post on X. “What only the closest people to me have known over the past four years is my time at DC Studios has been trying, to say the least,” Gunn wrote. “I dislike the at-times corporate nature of the job (thanks to Peter for taking the brunt of that). I’m always a very hard worker, but as a filmmaker I would at least get to take short breaks to unwind, and I’ve stolen even that time from both myself and my loved ones over the past four years.” He closed by saying that he was excited to return to working purely as a creator.

Gunn’s habit of engaging with fans directly on social media raised eyebrows at Warner Bros. and, later, at Skydance. But it cut both ways, with Gunn often using his vast following to promote upcoming projects and to engage more directly with fans in a way that inspired loyalty. Insiders also credit Safran and Gunn with not only reinvigorating Superman on screen, but also backing critically acclaimed shows such as “Lanterns” and “Peacemaker.” And the pair handled a delicate situation with the production of “The Batman: Part II,” which was delayed because of personal issues involving its writer and director Matt Reeves, a situation that ultimately required Steven Soderbergh to step in to finish the sequel under Reeves’ guidance. It is expected to wrap production in the next few weeks.

Insiders also note that Gunn and Safran didn’t have a chance to fully realize their vision of making superhero films featuring a wider variety of characters across a greater diversity of genres made for a broader range of budgets. Gunn was already plotting his next move even as he edited “Man of Tomorrow,” which features a team-up of Lex Luthor and Superman. He had outlined a third “Superman” film and was working on a “Justice League” and a “Batman” film, either one of which was expected to be his next project.

In Hollywood, however, timing is everything. And Gunn and Safran had the misfortune of trying to build their own MCU just as comic book movies were declining in popularity. Will Latcham have more success? He’s widely liked and boasts ties to the Christian community that could broaden DC’s core audience. But he also doesn’t come with the kind of pedigree and profile that Gunn did.

As for DC’s fans, they are bracing for yet another reboot having seen the comic book brand they love go through three different regimes over the last decade. Will Latcham provide the kind of consistency the company so desperately needs, or will he be just the latest in a long line of executives asked to pull off a miracle without being granted much of a runway?

Jaguars K Cam Little is the AFC special teams player of the week

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jaguars kicker Cam Little has gained a reputation for long field goals over his three years in the NFL, but he didn’t have to show off his big leg against the Ravens last Thursday.

Little was called on to kick four field goals during the game, but none of them were longer than 47 yards. Little made all of them and he also hit all three extra points he tried in Jacksonville’s 33-10 win.

On Wednesday, the NFL announced that Little has been named the AFC special teams player of the week. Little also won that award once last season.

Little is 14-of-16 on field goals and 25-of-26 for extra points over the entire season.

Scarlet Witchs return is everything thats wrong with the MCU

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The biggest story out of VisionQuest's finale wasn't the long-awaited reunion between twins Tommy and Billy Maximoff (Ruaridh Mollica and Joe Locke). Nor was it Vision (Paul Bettany) and Ultron's (James Spader) touching heart-to-heart about grief. Instead, it was the resurrection of Wanda Maximoff (Elizabeth Olsen) at the hands of Dr. Doom (Robert Downey Jr.), which occurs during the second of VisionQuest's two mid-credits scenes.

In theory, the return of Scarlet Witch is an exciting one, as she's one of the most powerful heroes in the MCU. Now that she's apparently under Dr. Doom's thrall, that will spell major trouble for the Avengers in Avengers: Doomsday. Powers aside, Wanda is also one of the MCU's most compelling characters, with Olsen's grief-stricken turn in WandaVision earning her an Emmy nomination.

Yet the reality of Wanda's return is that it, like much of the MCU post-Avengers: Endgame, is downright exhausting.

Wanda is the latest MCU mainstay to return from the dead. In addition to the return of everyone who got blipped by Thanos, characters like Loki (Tom Hiddleston) and Gamora (Zoe Saldaña) both stuck around after their deaths in Avengers: Infinity War thanks to some timeline meddling. Vision's complicated return also resulted in three MCU spin-off shows.

For Doomsday, Marvel is also bringing back characters and performers who received fitting send-offs in Avengers: Endgame. Chris Evans is returning as Steve Rogers, again likely due to timeline meddling, while Robert Downey Jr. is back, this time as Dr. Doom.

While I'm often excited to see these characters, Wanda included, the trend of constantly bringing everyone back is a tiring one. It's proof that the MCU has lost all sense of stakes: No one can be killed. Anyone can come back to be smashed together in a massive crossover event like Doomsday.

These constant returns also retroactively cheapen the most impressive feat in the MCU: Tony Stark's (Downey Jr.) mass resurrection of everyone who's been blipped. When half the universe, including some of Marvel's most beloved heroes, turned to ash at the end of Infinity War, it marked Marvel's heaviest moment to date. To reverse it would require a similarly heavy sacrifice, leading to Tony's death. Now, though, Marvel is bringing everyone back with the ease of a Dr. Doom spell, or some timey-wimey hand-waving. Next thing you know, Tony will be back. At this point, why not?

Keeping up with all the intricacies of the MCU has always been a daunting task, but it truly became a chore after Endgame. Now, instead of just watching Marvel's films, viewers also had to keep up with each TV show in order to understand what the hell was going on.

VisionQuest was already a fairly egregious example of this, as audiences had to have a solid understanding of WandaVision and Agatha All Along prior to watching, not to mention 2015's Avengers: Age of Ultron. It exacerbates the problem with its mid-credits scene, which treats Wanda's resurrection — one of the biggest MCU moments in recent years — as a simple teaser, instead of a pivotal story beat that deserves more fleshing out. Will Doomsday dive deeper into Dr. Doom's journey to resurrect Wanda, or will it just skip over it entirely and carry on with her being alive as if nothing's happened?

If so, that will be extra confusing for the casual Doomsday viewer who might not be aware of Wanda's return. Then again, they might have forgotten about her death, because there have been 10 other movies to keep track of in the four years between Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness and Doomsday.

Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness certainly did Wanda dirty with her villain arc and choice to sacrifice herself, so a part of me is ready to embrace what could be a new, more fulfilling arc for her. Yet a larger part of me is simply ambivalent, as I have been for much of Marvel's Phase Four and beyond. If you can't trust your own dramatic choices to stick, how can you expect me to care?

VisionQuest is now streaming on Disney+. Avengers: Doomsday hits theaters Dec. 18. Recommended deals for you HBO Max — top pick for premium content Disney+ — best for families Hulu — popular choice for originals Peacock — low-cost choice for live sport

The 10 Bleakest Christmas Movies, Ranked

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Christmas may be the holliest, jolliest, brightest time of the year, but it can also be the darkest, coldest, and most bleak as well. It's that wonderful dichotomy that makes it such a fertile time to set movies in. That has inspired a great many Christmas stories that are uplifting, even where they're also surprisingly dark, like It's a Wonderful Life or A Christmas Carol. It's also inspired some movies that only use the holiday as a backdrop for all sorts of dark, dreadful content.

The bleakest Christmas movies offer a bitter antidote to all the saccharine sweetness that overloads the most wonderful time of the year. They make us feel bad for a few hours so that we can then put our own lives into perspective. Even though 2026 has been one mean mother of a year so far for many, and the holiday season doesn't yet seem to have anything to offer as respite, we can at least take comfort in being miserable together. And we can do it while watching the ten bleakest Christmas movies.

At this point, there are more bleak movies featuring Silent Night in the title than there are festive ones. There's an entire horror franchise that started with a killer in a Santa Claus suit, a violent John Woo action revenge thriller, and this apocalyptic dark comedy where a Christmas party is pretense for mass suicide. Christmas parties have served as ironic settings for all kinds of dark occurrences in alternative holiday movies, and while Silent Night isn't quite as sharp as it wants to be, it's still the bleakest party you can attend this season.

Nell (Keira Knightley) and Simon (Matthew Goode) are hosting their annual Christmas party at their country home. It's also their final Christmas party since an ecological disaster is sweeping across the globe, killing everyone in its path. That's why all the attendees have taken a page out of Jim Jones' book and plan on committing suicide before the bell tolls. As the directorial debut for Camille Griffin, Silent Night uses its premise to poke fun at polite society in a bleakly funny way. It's a twisted holiday party that's likely more bearable than another political debate with your far-right relatives.

If this isn't the bleakest Christmas movie ever made, it's definitely the most brutal. A horror film which is often categorized as part of the New French Extremity, Inside was directed by Alexandre Bustillo and Julien Maury. Its story is centered around a birth on Christmas Eve, but instead of some hopeful Nativity-inspired narrative, it's a relentless, ultraviolent survival story. Like many other films of the same cinematic movement, Inside is a nasty, cruel piece of work that is definitively not meant for everyone. It takes a strong stomach to make it through this film, but it will definitely put your Christmas woes into perspective.

Sarah (Allyson Paradis), is a recently widowed woman who is set to give birth on Christmas Day. She plans to spend the eve before alone, having become withdrawn due to her grief. That's when a strange Woman (Béatrice Dalle) comes knocking at her door, demanding Sarah give her the unborn baby. What follows is a very red Christmas that even the most seasoned gorehounds may have trouble with. Inside is a truly bleak slasher movie that turns the miracle of birth into a holiday nightmare.

Spending time with your significant other's family is a part of the holidays that we all have to accept, and while for some that might mean enduring a bit of casual racism peppered into a long-winded story, it's got nothing on the tension that comes with being the new stepmom to a couple of kids who resent you. Add in a remote cabin in the woods and there's a recipe for a very bleak Christmas. That's the set-up for The Lodge, a psychological horror film from directors Veronika Franz and Severin Fiala, best known for their debut film Goodnight Mommy.

Grace (Riley Keough) is a former cult member and soon-to-be stepmother of two children who she's spending the Christmas holiday with in the titular location. When her fiancée leaves for work, Grace begins to experience strange occurrences, which she can't be sure whether are supernatural in nature, or the work of the children. The Lodge takes a couple of twists down its snowy path toward a ending that is as bleak as the frigid weather in the film. It's an atmospheric horror film anchored by Keough's bravura performance.

Elaine (Eva Birthistle) and Jonah (Stephen Campbell Moore) are spending the holidays with family along with their two young children and teenage daughter. Things take a swift turn towards violence as a virus infects the adolescents and they begin attacking with ferocity. The Children knows exactly which buttons it's pushing with its sickening premise, and it pushes harder and harder as the movie goes on. It's a twisted horror movie that proves that family can be murder around the holidays.

Family drives during the holidays can be hellish, but Dead End takes it to a more literal level. This underrated supernatural horror movie has a Twilight Zone-esque premise that heads toward an inevitably macabre ending. Directed by Jean-Baptise Andrea and Fabrice Canepa, and starring genre stalwarts Ray Wise and Lin Shaye, it's a bleak but darkly entertaining horror movie for the holidays.

While on a drive on Christmas Eve, a family led by Frank (Wise) finds themselves lost in the woods on a seemingly endless road. Their path is beset further by a mysterious woman in white and a hearse that's in pursuit of them. Dead End doesn't do anything spectacular or particularly original with its genre elements, but it's simple and effective, and definitely the bleakest holiday road trip.

Director Álex de la Iglesia makes films that often defy easy summation or categorization. Similarly, their tones can be just as equally indefinable. Most of his films have some degree of comedy to them, but they are also often grotesque, violent and unremittingly bleak. The Day of the Beast is no different. It's a satanic horror comedy that's absurd and outrageous in its approach to Christmas, with a sardonic mood that belies its bleak narrative.

Father Ángel (Álex Angulo) is a priest who heads to Madrid to commit as many sins as he can manage, in the hopes that it will allow him to gain access to the pending birth of the Antichrist so he can commit satanic infanticide. He's eventually aided in his quest by a heavy metal music fan and an occult television host. If you're looking to put Christ back into Christmas, The Day of the Beast isn't the right movie for you, but if you're looking for an irreverently bleak vision of faith, it's a gift from Hell.

You may not remember Christmas playing a big part in David Fincher's adaptation of Stieg Larsson's The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo, but the holiday actually bookends this bleak thriller. The film was also infamously marketed as the "Feel Bad Movie of Christmas," since it also released during the holiday season, and it fully lives up to the promise of that tagline. Filled with horrific acts of violence perpetrated against women, this chilly thriller is completely uninterested in making you feel excited or enthralled by its bloody action. It wants to rub your nose in it and then send you back out into the cold.

Lisbeth Salander (Rooney Mara) is the titular character, a skilled hacker who's endured a lifetime of abuse who finds a way to enact cathartic justice when she's recruited by disgraced newspaperman Mikael Blomkvist (Daniel Craig) to help him find a killer of women on a secluded island. Fincher's meticulous and cold direction meshes perfectly with Larsson's unflinching narrative, making this the definitive film version of the novel. It was supposed to be followed by sequels, but it turns out American audiences weren't ready to embrace such a bleak Christmas blockbuster.

No genre does bleak like noir, and when it comes to Christmas noirs, there's none bleaker, or better, than Blast of Silence. This low-budget hitman thriller, directed by and starring Allen Baron, is set in New York City during the holiday season, and utilizes both its mean streets and its bright lights to great discordant effect. Between its bleak visuals and blunt violence, the movie means to leave a bad taste in your mouth in the most noir way possible.

Frankie Bono (Baron) is a hitman who has come home to the Big Apple at Christmastime not to see family, but to kill a stranger. Between plotting his kill, Bono reconnects with some past acquaintances and deals with some less than savory underworld types. There's an authentic grittiness to the film, and Baron makes for a suitably scabrous lead. He's an anti-social man of violence, with a second-person narration that runs through his head. Blast of Silence is rough around its edges, doesn't pull its punches and ends on a suitably dark note.

Slashers are nothing new under the Christmas tree. There have been plenty of psycho Santas, killer snowmen and masked maniacs over the years. Even the seminal Black Christmas has three separate editions. While the 2006 remake is sleazy as hell, and the 2019 version gave it a Me Too makeover, there's nothing that can compete with Bob Clark's bleak original. It's a totemic slasher film which had a huge influence on John Carpenter's Halloween, and it's just as twisted.

Set at a sorority house during a Christmas Party, the film follows the house sisters as they are increasingly harassed by an anonymous caller, who, unbeknownst to them, has also taken up residence in their attic. As the night goes on, more and more of the women are stalked and killed, while the police ineffectually try to help. There's a strong feminist undercurrent to Black Christmas, which is more subversive and intelligent than many of the slashers that would follow in its wake. That the women it portrays are such well-rounded and independent characters only makes their demises that much more effective. Between this film and A Christmas Story, Clark covered the entire spectrum of Christmas movies, from happy and bright to hopeless and bleak.

Things are always bleak in a dystopia, even when it's Christmas, and even when that dystopia comes with a heavy helping of satire. Terry Gilliam's magnum opus Brazil is set around the Christmas holiday, which highlights the consumerist society which is run by inefficient corporate bureaucrats. It also meshes well with the whimsical nature of the movie, which features several fantasy sequences and absurdist depictions of its industrial future world. Despite, or perhaps because of, the humor and whimsy, the film is still unremittingly bleak, depicting a world where the government-sponsored torture and death of an innocent man comes down to a typo.

Sam Lowry (Jonathan Pryce) is a sad little cog in the massive machinery of the totalitarian government who dreams of being a hero with wings who saves a distressed damsel. When he sees a woman who resembles the damsel of his dreams, his pursuit of her turns him into a fugitive of the state. Filled with the kind of outlandish imagery Gilliam is known for, Brazil influenced a decade's worth of dystopian cities on film, and its sobering vision of the future has only become more disconcerting for many viewers who fear we're edging toward it as a reality. Executives at Universal Pictures at one time wanted to cut the film's infamously sad ending, but it remains intact as the perfect punctuation for one of the bleakest Christmas movies ever made.

Lightdox Acquires Sales Rights To IDFA World Premiere ‘The Big Boom!’, Gabriel Tejedor Film With Explosive Contradictions

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Lightdox has acquired world sales rights to The Big Boom! ahead of its world premiere in the Frontlight section of International Documentary Festival Amsterdam (IDFA).

Swiss filmmaker Gabriel Tejedor directed the feature documentary that explores “the contradictions of a Bulgarian town pulled into Europe’s shifting geopolitical order, where arms production for Ukraine’s front lines collides with enduring ties to Russia, personal convictions, and the everyday need to survive.”

The Big Boom! premieres at IDFA on Saturday. Watch the film’s trailer below. Related Stories Documentary Riz Ahmed Boards Elham Ehsas' Short Film 'Forgotten Spaceman' About Afghanistan's First Astronaut Documentary IDFA Announces Competition Lineups, Adds 'Musk' And Elizabeth Holmes Documentary 'You Can See Everything'

The documentary contrasts ancient traditions in Kazanlak, Bulgaria’s “Valley of Roses,” with the town’s major industry – the Arsenal weapons factory. That defense contractor, founded in the 19th century, “provides work for thousands of people manufacturing weapons and ammunition,” according to a synopsis. “Through the lives of four residents, Tejedor examines the place this industry occupies in the town’s economy and identity, and the questions it raises for those whose lives depend on it.” Watch on Deadline

“The Big Boom! brings us face to face with a contradiction at the heart of today’s Europe – a town making the weapons of war while struggling with its own loyalties and beliefs,” commented Lightdox CEO Anna Berthollet. “Gabriel Tejedor finds a way into this reality that feels both clear-eyed and deeply human. As someone of Bulgarian origin myself, I find his sensitive portrayal of Kazanlak and its people particularly meaningful. He reveals the layers of history, identity and geopolitical influence that shape the region without reducing them to a political argument. We’re delighted to bring this timely film to audiences worldwide.”

The Big Boom! is the fifth feature directed by Tejedor, the filmmaker and journalist “whose work explores utopias and the relationship between individuals and authority across the former Soviet Union and its satellite states.”

“I have never been interested in war or weapons. But the invasion of Ukraine shattered my convictions,” Tejedor said in a statement. “Ever since, one question has haunted me: what does it mean to be a pacifist today? I do not judge the workers at Arsenal, nor the local population. The Big Boom! does not seek to lecture, but to confront the grey zones of a continent trapped in its own paradoxes.”

The film is a production of Mira Film Basel GmbH, co-produced by RTS Radio Télévision Suisse, SRG SSR, with the support of Swiss and regional film funds and cultural institutions.

Susanna Guggenberger produced The Big Boom! Cinematography is by Thomas Szczepanski; Christine Hoffet edited the film. The score is composed by Petar Dundakov.

Dark Horse Estate, Where Taylor Swift and Dolly Parton Recorded Hits, Is Listed for $19.5 Million

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Dark Horse Estate, home to a famous recording studio in Franklin, TN, where artists such as Taylor Swift and Dolly Parton have recorded hit music, is now available for $19.5 million.

The estate was built in 1998 on a sprawling 10.01-acre lot. It was where more than 65 gold and platinum albums have been recorded and features 23,138 square feet of interior space, including a manor, the recording studio, The Cottage, The Bungalow apartment, and The Party Barn.

According to the listing, legendary artists such as Keith Urban, Reba McEntire, Amy Grant, and Peter Frampton have come here to “escape, focus, and create."

The retreat has multiple studios, private guest quarters, and public gathering spaces. It has also reached the top of the week’s most popular homes list.

The outdoor spaces are designed for entertaining and creative inspiration. More than 3,000 square feet of covered porches and walkways overlook the peaceful property.

Other properties grabbing attention this week include a transformed schoolhouse in Kansas, a Virginia farmhouse built next to an accessible cave that once housed a moonshine bootlegging operation, and a South Carolina estate perched on dramatic rock formations in the foothills of the Blue Ridge Mountains.

Why it’s here: Here’s a chance to buy the famous pink-hued Barbiecore Castle located near the St. Croix River. It currently operates as a “high-demand” short-term rental.

The sellers purchased the seven-bedroom Victorian for $614,000 in 2020 and added the iconic home to their portfolio of unique rentals. The pretty pad's 5,189-square-foot interior has fuchsia walls, trim, staircase, treads, ceilings, and built-ins, along with Barbie decor.

Beyond the eye-popping design, there are two full kitchens, some original woodwork, a yellow spiral staircase, a movie room, and a primary suite with a walk-in closet. The fenced lot has a hot tub, a swim spa, an entertaining patio, a two-car garage, and a driveway with space for 12 vehicles.

If you’ve ever wanted your own Barbie Dreamhouse, the home could easily be converted into a single-family residence or used for multigenerational living.

Jennifer R Martin of Edina Realty Inc. - Woodbury is the listing agent.

Why it’s here: This thoughtfully updated 1750s stone country estate sits on 3.02 acres of “renewed” grounds.

The eight-bedroom home has been extensively renovated to meet modern standards, with a new roof, new porches, updated electrical and plumbing systems, new heating and central air conditioning, and refinished interiors. The 6,750-square-foot, three-level interior has two kitchens, dual living rooms, an art studio, and a two-bedroom guest suite. Historic highlights include custom millwork, preserved hardwood floors, built-in cabinetry, a grand staircase, and multiple fireplaces.

A wraparound terrace overlooks the private lot, which has a custom-built fire pit and a barn.

Christopher Waaben of Maximum Realty Services is the listing agent.

Why it’s here: The former Hinerville Schoolhouse has been converted into stylish living quarters while keeping a nod to its past.

Built in 1898, the 1,546-square-foot limestone building has many original architectural details, including a belfry, beadboard walls and ceilings, a century-old blackboard, vaulted ceilings, hardwood floors, limestone details, and even the school bell, which can still be rung. The landmark one-room classroom, currently used as a “highly successful short-term rental,” now has an open-concept floor plan with a great room with a fireplace, a kitchen with modern appliances and a breakfast bar, three bedrooms, and two bathrooms, including one with a claw-foot tub.

A large deck designed for entertaining overlooks the 1.57-acre lot. According to the listing, all of the furnishings can be included “with an acceptable offer” for a turnkey opportunity.

Levi Whitebread of Re/Max Signature Properties is the listing agent.

Why it’s here: This 13-acre farmstead was originally part of a 500-acre farm. It's seeking a buyer to restore its 130-year-old farmhouse to its former glory.

Built in 1896, the 1,416-square-foot home has original wood walls, ceilings, and floors, and a relocated kitchen that was once where the bathroom now is. One bedroom is in the original portion of the home, while two more, added in the 1920s, have private access from an exterior porch. The property has an original Aermotor Chicago windmill that once operated the farm’s well, plus an additional well and septic system where a mobile home previously stood.

According to the listing, the property consists of two parcels, and the second might offer future buildable potential. It is being sold as is.

Katie Garrett of Garrett Realty is the listing agent.

Jonathan Ross Explains Backstory Behind “Tasteless” David Walliams Joke

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

British TV presenter Jonathan Ross has landed himself in some hot water over the past week for his past association with both David Walliams and Russell Brand.

The Guardian and ITV News’ report into allegations against Walliams including from teenagers referenced a joke Ross made at the 2004 British Comedy Awards. “The comedy awards is 15 years old – 15 years old! And like most 15-year-olds, it’s drunk, it’s unpredictable and it’s probably going home with David Walliams,” Ross joked at the time.

Ross addressed the joke and the broader allegations this morning, saying in a statement he is “as horrified by the revelations about David Walliams and the crimes of Russell Brand as the rest of the nation.” Related Stories News Comedian Lucy Beaumont Fears Being Blacklisted By TV Industry After Blowing Whistle On David Walliams News David Walliams Being Cut From New Movie 'Fing!' Following Allegations; Comcast-Owned Sky Exits UK Distribution Of Film

Ross said the “tasteless joke that I would not dream of making today about anyone” was submitted to him by the Comedy Awards writing team at the time. Watch on Deadline

“I believed that the punchline was a reference to the fact he had publicly dated younger women, exaggerated for comic effect,” said Ross. “Had I had any reason to suspect that it might be referring to actual events, I would not only have refused to use it, but I would not have felt comfortable continuing a personal or professional relationship with him. I also like to think that I would have acted upon that knowledge.”

Ross’ association with Walliams and Brand has been scrutinized in recent days.

Along with the Comedy Awards joke, The Guardian published an image of Ross with Walliams at a charity quiz. In the photo, Ross is sat at a table while Walliams has his arm around an 18-year-old who accused Walliams of “pleading with me to share a bed with him” and “putting his head in my lap and making me stroke his head.” There is no suggestion that Ross was aware of any of the behavior Walliams has been accused of.

Ross’ past association with Brand, who was jailed earlier this week for 10-and-a-half years for rape and sexual assault, has also come up in recent days. The pair created scandal in 2008 for prank calling Fawlty Towers actor Andrew Sachs and making lewd comments about his granddaughter, who had previously been in a relationship with Brand.

After trying to have the articles blocked via the courts, the Walliams allegations dominated the news agenda yesterday and have called into question a culture that previously allowed men like Walliams and Brand to flourish. Culture Secretary Lisa Nandy said “the allegations are the latest in a long line of reported abuses of power in an industry where we have known for so long that the power imbalances are huge.”

Walliams has been dropped by his agent and his shows including Little Britain have been removed from BBC iPlayer. He is accused of using his celebrity to prey on women as young as 15, making contact with them through work, including on Britain’s Got Talent, to pursue them for sexual purposes.

Deadline has contacted Walliams’ legal representatives for comment. In a statement to ITV News, Walliams denied all allegations against him. When trying to prevent publication of the articles, Walliams’ lawyers revealed that he had made two attempts to take his life because of the allegations.

“I am not a sexual predator… I have not broken the law,” Walliams said. “I am devastated that some of the women I met ten to twenty years ago have now expressed hurt or anger towards me.”

He continued: “Looking back, I realise that at times I behaved thoughtlessly towards other people I had casual sexual relationships with… I realise I did not always take people’s feelings properly into account. That is something I regret. And whilst a power imbalance is inevitable when one party is famous, abusing that power is something else entirely and it is something that I never did.”

Aaron Rodgers is one win away from tying Drew Brees for fourth-most of all time

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If the Steelers beat the Bengals on Monday night, Aaron Rodgers will move into a tie for fourth time on the NFL’s all-time wins list.

Rodgers’ teams have won 180 games he has started, counting the playoffs. His next win will give him No. 181, the same number Drew Brees retired with, and the fourth most of all time.

The only quarterbacks in NFL history who have won more starts are Tom Brady with a preposterous 286 wins, Peyton Manning with 200 and Brett Favre with 199. Rodgers has said this will be his last NFL season, so he won’t catch Favre for third place.

Rodgers won 158 games as starting quarterback for the Packers, six with the Jets and has now won 16 with the Steelers.

Stacy Keach, ‘Mike Hammer’ and ‘Fat City’ Star, Dead at 85

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Stacy Keach, the actor whose turns as Hamlet on stage and hard-bitten detective Mike Hammer on television captivated audiences, died on Tuesday in Magdalenka, Poland, at 85.

His death was confirmed by his wife, Malgosia Tomassi Keach, who said he died at his family home. “After a long and difficult battle with a serious heart condition and heart failure, Stacy peacefully departed this world,” she said in a statement to The Hollywood Reporter. “He fought for so long, with extraordinary courage, determination and strength. He was a true warrior, facing his illness with the same remarkable spirit and resilience that defined his life.”

She continued, “Stacy was not only a legendary actor, admired and loved by audiences the world over, but also my beloved husband, my companion and the heart of our family. His passing leaves an immeasurable emptiness in our lives. But his extraordinary spirit, his unforgettable voice and the love he gave so generously will remain with us forever.”

Walter Stacy Keach Jr. was born on June 2, 1941, in Savannah, Georgia. His father was veteran character actor Stacy Keach Sr. and his mother was Mary Cain Peckham, the daughter of Texas oilman William H. Peckham. Along with his wife and their son, Shannon, and daughter, Karolina, he is also survived by his brother, actor James Keach. The siblings portrayed outlaw James brothers and also served as their own co-producers on the 1980 western The Long Riders.

Keach received a Tony nomination for portraying Buffalo Bill in the 1969 Broadway production Indians and went on to gain further acclaim as the fedora-wearing Hammer on the CBS series that ran from 1984 to 1987. He reprised the role in the 1989 TV movie Mike Hammer: Murder Takes All and another show, Mike Hammer, Private Eye, from 1997 to 1998.

Throughout his prolific career, Keach co-starred in a wide range of projects from movies The New Centurions, the John Huston-directed Fat City, and two Cheech and Chong films, Up in Smoke (1978) and Nice Dreams (1981). His depiction of Ernest Hemingway in the 1988 made-for-TV movie Hemingway earned him a Golden Globe Award.

He also won many stage accolades including Helen Hayes Awards in 1991 and 1996 for his work in Richard III and Macbeth with the Shakespeare Theatre Company. In 2015, he was inducted into the American Theatre Hall of Fame and honored with a star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame in 2019. Trending Stories Sofia Franklyn Reveals the Dark Side of ‘Call Her Daddy’ in Explosive Memoir: 9 Biggest Bombshells Judge Sets Steven Tyler Sex Assault Trial Date Paul McCartney and Ringo Starr to Reunite at Las Vegas Show in 2027 Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’

While Keach never reached superstardom, his career reflected his profound passion for both his craft and life. “If a script interests me, or a character intrigues me, or the cast features someone I’ve always wanted to work with, or the location is somewhere exotic, then sign me up,” Keach penned in his memoir All in All: An Actor’s Life On and Off the Stage. “I’m not going to wait for a call that may or may not come.”

8 Movies To Watch if You Love Ken Russell's 'The Devils'

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 17th-century France, Loudun became the site of the Loudun possessions, a gruesome case of alleged mass demonic possession that is now understood as a landmark study in mass hysteria. The "possessions" were blamed on Urbain Grandier, a charismatic but womanizing local priest who was then executed for political purposes. This event became the inspiration for Aldous Huxley's 1952 non-fiction book The Devils of Loudun, and then John Whiting's 1960 stage play The Devils. In 1971, Ken Russell took both of these works as inspiration and made The Devils.

One of the most notoriously heavily-censored horror movies in film history, The Devils was massively controversial upon release for its graphic sexual imagery and blasphemous content, but has since come to be recognized as one of the greatest films of its time. With the recent release of the 4K restoration of the film's 114-minute director's cut, now's about as perfect a time as any to watch the eight most noteworthy films that feel remarkably similar to The Devils for one reason or another.

As far as deliberately and calculatedly blasphemous films go, it doesn't get much more iconic than Viridiana, made by one of the most notoriously anti-clerical auteurs in film history, Luis Buñuel. Arguably the greatest of Buñuel's Mexican movies, Viridiana is one of the greatest movie classics of 1961, following a devout novice nun who abandons her vows to shelter local vagrants after the suicide of her fetishistic uncle.

Its surrealistic imagery is about as brilliant as you'd expect from any Buñuel film.

A must-see for all those whose favorite part of The Devils is the scathing study of The Profane as a philosophical concept, Viridiana is an unforgiving critique of religious dogma and naive idealism. Its surrealistic imagery is about as brilliant as you'd expect from any Buñuel film, and Silvia Pinal's lead performance is a tour de force. It's definitely more overtly sacrilegious than The Devils, but it's every bit as much of a hyper-influential must-see.

Before The Wizard of Oz came around to revolutionize fantasy cinema forever, there were many fantasy films that are also still remembered among the greatest of all time, including the horror essay film Häxan. This silent Swedish masterpiece is a semi-documentary blends scholarly literature and phantasmagoric horror, charting the history of witchcraft and Satanism from the Middle Ages to the 20th century.

It's one of the clearest and strongest cinematic ancestors to Ken Russell's magnum opus, and one of the most important documentaries in the history of cinema. Insightful without ever feeling didactic and atmospheric without ever sacrificing its scholarly dimensions, Häxan laid the foundations for horror cinema to explore themes of religious hysteria, state control through religion, and institutional persecution. Both through its visuals and its mere premise, it was far ahead of its time.

Robin Hardy's The Wicker Man is one of the greatest and most influential British horror movies of the 20th century, as well as one of the most important folk horror films of all time. It's based on David Pinner's horror novel Ritual, and it follows a devout Christian cop who travels to a remote Scottish island to investigate a young girl's disappearance, only to discover that the local pagan community has lured him there as a human sacrifice for a failing harvest.

Much like The Devils, The Wicker Man explores how sexual repression can be weaponized and exploited by oppressive forces; and much like The Devils, it forces the sacred and the profane to clash visually, narratively, and thematically throughout. It's one of those movies unlike anything you've ever seen before, a genre-defining masterpiece that shows how dogmatic self-righteousness can be little more than a self-destructive trap.

Four franchises, four completely different ways of getting underneath your skin. This is the one built out of your particular fear, why it works so well on you, and what it would leave behind.

Your nightmare does not wait outside the door. It waits for you to close your eyes, which you are going to have to do eventually, because nobody has ever won that particular argument.

Your nightmare is not really the doll. It is the face of the person you tell, and the way they decide, very kindly, that you have imagined all of it.

Your nightmare has your number, knows your name, and has almost certainly been inside your house before. The worst part is that you already know them.

Your nightmare is not really about you. It is about the people asleep down the hall, and the feeling that whatever is here is not interested in leaving.

What better recommendation for people who loved Ken Russell's The Devils than the rest of Russell's impeccable filmography? As it happens, after making one of the best horror movies of the 1970s, he made one of the best hard sci-fi movies of the 1980s: Altered States, based on Paddy Chayefsky's 1978 novel. In it, a Harvard psychophysiologist uses sensory deprivation tanks and powerful tribal hallucinogens to explore alternate states of consciousness, unwittingly triggering a dangerous devolution into a primitive ape-man.

Brilliantly experimental and surrealistic from beginning to end, Altered States is anchored by unparalleled visuals and sound design, creating a true aesthetic supernova further bolstered by John Corigliano's avant-garde score. Like all auteurs worth their salt, Russell had a collection of themes that were most interesting to him over the course of his career, and Altered States thus explores many ideas similar to those found in The Devils. Instead of 17th-century religion and politics, this film explores 20th-century scientific hubris, exploring ideas of repressed biological impulses through intentionally grotesque images.

Chilean-French avant-garde auteur Alejandro Jodorowsky is one of the biggest and most influential names in the history of surrealist cinema, and there are many who would point to The Holy Mountain as his magnum opus. After the underground success of the acid Western El Topo, Beatles manager Allen Klein agreed to produce Holy Mountain, and John Lennon and Yoko Ono also contributed financially. The result is one of the most delectably strange and fascinatingly impenetrable Mexican movies of all time, about seven people led up a sacred mountain by a powerful alchemist so they can steal the secret of immortality.

The Holy Mountain is certainly not for those who prefer their fantasy movies straightforward, and it's probably not the most accessible introduction to cinematic surrealism for those unfamiliar; but it's a must-see masterpiece nonetheless, especially for those who love the provocative blasphemy and hyper-stylized colors of The Devils. One of the best international masterpieces of all time, The Holy Mountain is just as fiercely anti-authoritarian as Russell's masterpiece, rejecting institutional corruption and repressive state control through a bunch of deliberately shocking moments.

There is perhaps no better Ken Russell film for people to watch after seeing (and loving) The Devils than Women in Love, which is also anchored by a powerhouse performance by Oliver Reed. This is not a horror movie, though it is a period piece, this one a romantic drama following the relationships between two sisters and two men in a mining town in post-World War I England. Based on the 1920 novel of the same title by D. H. Lawrence, the film obtained an impressive four Academy Award nominations, including Glenda Jackson's victory in the Best Actress category.

This was the movie that established Russell as a major force in world cinema, and it was its huge commercial and critical success that gave Warner the confidence to give Russell a blank check to make The Devils. These two films couldn't possibly be more different in terms of tone, with Women in Love being an uncompromisingly dense and philosophically profound adaptation of the source material. However, since Women in Love directly preceded The Devils, you can see many of the blueprints for Russell's next film there: eroticism, theatricality, and characters trapped between society's expectations and their own physical and psychological impulses.

Directed by Pier Paolo Pasolini and loosely adapted from the 1785 novel The 120 Days of Sodom by the Marquis de Sade, Salò, or the 120 Days of Sodom is perhaps one of the most controversial major films in the history of cinema. Taking the basis of the Marquis de Sade's story from 18th-century France to World War II Italy, it's about four wealthy fascists who kidnap a group of teenagers and subject them to 120 days of systemic mental, physical, and sexual torture inside a secluded villa. For its unapologetic approach to topics that continue to be taboo today, the film has been widely banned and censored across the world throughout history.

It isn't the least bit hard to see why. Much like The Devils, this is one of the heaviest movies of the 20th century, an absolutely unrelenting political allegory of fascism's cold cruelty shot with high-art anti-sensationalism that nonetheless doesn't make the film any easier to stomach. It's a remarkable feat that Pasolini was able to take such an inflammatory premise and manage to treat it with such care that it doesn't feel exploitative at all, a compliment that can also be directed at The Devils.

There are only two major feature films directly depicting the Loudun possessions, and the first one came out a decade before The Devils. It is, of course, the Polish horror art film Mother Joan of the Angels, directed by Jerzy Kawalerowicz and based on a novella of the same title by Jarosław Iwaszkiewicz. Winner of the Special Jury Prize at the 1961 Cannes Film Festival, Mother Joan of the Angels is one of those near-perfect horror movies that nobody remembers anymore, but which should be essential viewing for anyone even slightly interested in the story and context of the Loudun possessions.

Most would agree that the film isn't quite as masterful, timeless, or hard-hitting as The Devils, but it's still very much worthy of praise. With some incredibly striking black-and-white cinematography that stands in stark contrast to the vibrancy of Russell's masterpiece, Lucyna Winnicka's transcendental performance as Mother Joan, and a tremendous level of intellectual and existential depth, Mother Joan of the Angels is to the '60s what The Devils is to the '70s. Both are among the greatest "demonic possession" movies in the history of horror cinema.

In Kirk Cousins, the Vikings landed their first choice

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After last night’s abysmal performance by the offense against the Bills, the Vikings mobilized to trade for a quarterback. Ultimately, they got the guy they wanted.

Per a source with knowledge of the situation, Kirk Cousins was Minnesota’s first choice.

The Vikings explored other options. Multiple players, including fellow former Vikings quarterback Daniel Jones.

Cousins’s history with the team makes it easier to get him up to speed. He had 25 regular-season games with coach Kevin O’Connell, and four seasons with receiver Justin Jefferson.

The existence of options may have helped the Vikings keep the compensation to only a third-round pick. Given the level of desperation, the Vikings seemed destined to give up more than that to fix a problem that threatened to implode the season.

That’s not an overstatement. The dam was breaking. Frustration was rampant during the debacle against the Bills, on both sides of the ball.

It was a simple proposition. If the Vikings didn’t trade for a quarterback on Tuesday, they could not be considered a serious franchise.

In the end, they managed to stop the bleeding by sending their third-round pick (their own, not the one they acquired from the Eagles for defensive end Jonathan Greenard) to the Raiders for Cousins.

It’s unclear how much better things will be with Cousins back in the fold. It can’t get worse than what they put on the field for Week 9.
